5. **Certainty level:** The post-failure vegetation-exposure mechanism is explicitly hedged ("suggests that," "may expose") — appropriately tentative since it is a plausible but not directly tested explanation for a secondary, non-central finding; the pre-failure mechanism (displacement-gradient/phase-aliasing) is stated with a lighter hedge ("likely associated with") and reinforced by the concordance citation.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** The post-failure reversal is a genuinely unexpected/discordant-with-expectation result (if coherence loss = instability, why would coherence INCREASE after the ground has already failed and presumably remains disturbed?) and the paper explains it via a distinct mechanism (vegetation clearing rather than continued ground disturbance) rather than leaving it as a puzzling inconsistency.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Direct forward-research call closing the subsection: "Further investigation into other case studies is needed to establish universal indicators and thresholds for landslides at critical stages" — an explicit, appropriately scoped generalizability caveat/research-agenda statement.
8. **Limitations bounding:** The closing sentence functions as an implicit single-case-study limitation (thresholds not yet established as universal), stated as a forward-looking research need rather than a defect.
9. **Role:** Core interpretive, discordant/reversed-pattern handling + repeated cross-method concordance validation + closing research-agenda statement for the subsection.

#### §5.4.1 "April 2015 to December 2017" — Paragraph 1
1. **Function:** Core interpretive — proposes a mechanistic explanation (progressive rupture-surface maturation, not yet self-sustaining) for the observed pattern of short transient creep events separated by long quiescent periods, and explicitly names a data-resolution limitation constraining what can be concluded about within-interval continuity.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The dimensionless activity index for Apr 2015-Dec 2017 (Fig. 6, Table 1): short-lived transient creep events, prolonged inactivity between them.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct mechanistic proposal, moderately hedged: "This **suggests** the presence at this stage of a maturing surface of rupture, allowing for some hydro-mechanical fluid-solid coupling (Agliardi et al., 2020), **but not yet** a self-sustaining process of progressive failure" — the mechanism is attributed to an established theoretical framework (Agliardi et al. 2020) but its application to this specific case is explicitly the paper's own inferential move, appropriately hedged as "suggests."
4. **Literature use:** Agliardi et al. (2020) supplies the general hydro-mechanical fluid-solid-coupling framework being applied here to a new instance — an A3-type attributed-mechanism-from-literature use, applied to explain a pattern the paper's own data shows but did not itself establish the underlying physics of.
5. **Certainty level:** Explicitly hedged ("suggests," "but not yet") — an appropriately tentative mechanistic interpretation of an early-stage, data-resolution-limited signal.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** N/A.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** None yet — sets up the temporal narrative that develops through the rest of §5.4.
8. **Limitations bounding:** An explicit, locally placed data-resolution limitation follows directly: "Displacements during this period may span up to 72 d due to sampling intervals... the temporal resolution of the data does not allow us to determine whether movement was continuous or intermittent within these intervals" — a precise, quantitatively stated (72-day/96-day gaps) limitation on temporal-pattern claims, paired with an honest statement of exactly what cannot be resolved (continuous vs. intermittent motion) rather than a vague caveat.
9. **Role:** Core interpretive/mechanistic (A3-type, literature-established mechanism applied to a new instance) + locally placed, precisely quantified limitation.

#### §5.4.1 — Paragraph 2
1. **Function:** Rival-hypothesis elimination and cross-site discordance handling — tests whether seismicity alone, or seismicity-plus-rainfall, explains the observed acceleration pattern; explicitly contrasts Achoma's response with a structurally similar comparator landslide (Maca) that behaved differently, and proposes a physical reason for the difference.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The August 2016 earthquake (highest PGA in the record) producing only modest, non-sustained movement; the 2016/2017 rainy season (comparable rainfall to later years) NOT triggering prolonged acceleration.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** A双-part elimination: seismicity alone is shown insufficient ("did result in some movement, it was lower than the movements observed between April 2015 and March 2016" — actually smaller than an earlier, non-seismic event), and rainfall alone is shown insufficient (comparable rainfall in later years without the same immediate effect). The paper then proposes a combined-factor explanation, hedged: "This **suggests that** a combination of seismicity and rainfall **may be more critical** for driving significant acceleration than either factor alone." A sharp cross-site discordance is then named directly: "**Unlike** the Maca landslide, which exhibited prolonged post-seismic accelerations following the [2016] earthquakes, lasting 5 months and several weeks, respectively (Bontemps et al., 2020), the Achoma landslide's response was transient and not sustained." This discordance is then explained mechanistically, again attributing the underlying general mechanism to prior literature and applying it as a candidate (not confirmed) explanation: "Post-seismic motion has been associated to pore-water pressure increases due to sediment contraction (Lacroix et al., 2022). The absence of post-seismic motion at the Achoma landslide **may reflect that** the rupture surface was not sufficiently mature, and lacked the necessary pathways for water to migrate... Without this migration, the pore pressure... **could not** build up to a level that would sustain post-seismic motion."
4. **Literature use:** Bontemps et al. (2020) supplies the discordant comparator case (Maca landslide) with specific, quantified contrast (5 months/several weeks of sustained post-seismic motion, vs. Achoma's transient response) — a clean, direct discordance-naming citation use, not softened or downplayed. Lacroix et al. (2022) supplies the general pore-pressure mechanism from prior literature, applied here (per A3) to explain why the mechanism did NOT operate as expected at Achoma — a distinctive variant: literature is used to explain an *absence* of an expected effect, not to explain a positive finding.
5. **Certainty level:** Multi-tiered hedging within one paragraph: the seismicity-plus-rainfall combined-driver claim is hedged ("suggests," "may be more critical"); the pore-pressure-migration explanation for the cross-site difference is explicitly and doubly hedged ("may reflect," "could not") — appropriately tentative since this is a mechanistic inference about subsurface hydro-mechanical conditions the paper's InSAR data cannot directly observe.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** This is the paper's clearest, most fully developed discordant-finding-handling paragraph — a specific, named, quantitatively contrasted comparator case (Maca/Bontemps et al. 2020) is used to sharpen rather than obscure the site-specific finding, and the paper proposes (without over-claiming to demonstrate) a physical reason for the site-to-site difference.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Implicit — reinforces the paper's broader argument that internal rupture-surface maturity, not external triggers alone, governs landslide response, a theme carried through to the final acceleration phase (§5.4.2).
8. **Limitations:** The pore-pressure-migration mechanism is explicitly flagged as inferential/untested by this paper's own data (InSAR cannot directly measure subsurface pore pressure), functioning as an implicit certainty-level limitation on this specific explanatory chain.
9. **Role:** Core interpretive, rival/comparator-discordance handling + doubly-hedged attributed-mechanism explanation for an absence-of-expected-effect.

#### §5.4.2 "December 2017 to June 2020" — Paragraph 1
1. **Function:** Core interpretive — identifies a behavioral regime shift (December 2017 onward) and proposes a mechanistic chain (basal-gouge formation → permeability/pore-pressure change → cyclic acceleration-deceleration-steady-state behavior), drawing on multiple external mechanistic frameworks applied to this new instance.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The shift to longer activity periods from December 2017, with a stronger/quicker response to the wet-season onset despite lower seismicity than the earlier period.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct interpretive statement ("suggesting that the rupture surface has fully developed and become more sensitive to perturbations"), followed by a chained, multi-source mechanistic explanation: "This likely reflects the formation of a fine-grained gouge along the basal shear plane (Agliardi et al., 2020), reducing permeability and altering pore pressure dynamics. These internal changes led to cycles of acceleration, deceleration, and steady-state stages (Zhou et al., 2018)..., driven by transient perturbations... and modulated by mechanisms like pore pressure dissipation (Lacroix et al., 2020) or strain-strengthening behaviour (Agliardi et al., 2020)."
4. **Literature use:** Agliardi et al. (2020) (cited twice, for gouge formation and for strain-strengthening behavior), Zhou et al. (2018) (for the acceleration-deceleration-steady-state cycle framework), Lacroix et al. (2020) (for pore-pressure dissipation) — four distinct mechanistic citations chained together to build a composite explanatory model for a pattern the paper's own InSAR data shows but whose underlying hydro-mechanical physics it cannot directly measure; a dense instance of the A3 attributed-mechanism pattern, here stacking multiple sources rather than relying on one.
5. **Certainty level:** The overall chain is stated with a single light hedge ("likely reflects") at its base, then proceeds fairly directly through the mechanistic steps — a moderate confidence level appropriate for a well-supported, literature-grounded mechanistic narrative applied to a new but structurally comparable case (progressive landslide failure is a well-studied phenomenon class).
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** N/A in this specific paragraph (concordant application of established theory).
7. **Interpretation→implication:** The paragraph closes by tying the mechanism directly back to an observed data pattern (sustained displacement through the 2018 dry season "despite the absence of significant external triggers"), reinforcing that internal processes (not just external forcing) drive the later-stage behavior — a finding with direct relevance to the paper's central early-warning argument (precursors can be internally driven and thus detectable independent of external trigger timing).
8. **Limitations:** None new in this specific paragraph, though the reliance on multiple external, non-directly-tested mechanistic frameworks is itself an implicit scope marker (this paper's own data shows the kinematic pattern; the underlying hydro-mechanical explanation is borrowed).
9. **Role:** Core interpretive/mechanistic, dense multi-source attributed-mechanism chaining (A3-type, most citation-dense single mechanistic paragraph in this paper).

#### §5.4.2 — Paragraph 2
1. **Function:** Core interpretive — compares the paper's own InSAR-derived acceleration-onset timing against the independent optical-method comparator (Lacroix et al. 2023), explicitly naming a timing discordance and reasoning about the sensitivity difference between the two methods; also (within the same paragraph) rules out rainfall as a sufficient standalone explanation via a within-study year-to-year comparison.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The paper's own dimensionless activity index showing acceleration onset as early as January 2020; Lacroix et al. (2023)'s optical-image-based dating of final-acceleration onset to March 2020.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct discordance statement without minimization: "While optical images from Lacroix et al. (2023) pinpoint the beginning of the final acceleration in March 2020, InSAR-based observations in this study **suggest that** the landslide began accelerating as early as January 2020." The paper does not explicitly state which method is "more correct," but the implicit framing (InSAR detecting an earlier onset) supports the paper's central novelty claim (its markers detect precursors optical methods miss) — a discordance handled as evidence FOR the paper's contribution, not against it. A second rival-explanation elimination follows: "Although rainfall was undoubtedly a key factor, similar rainfall totals in 2017 and 2018 did not lead to failure, **suggesting that** other factors, such as accumulated damage or changes in the basal shear zone, were more critical in 2020" — a within-study, own-data comparison (not literature-based) ruling out rainfall-magnitude-alone as sufficient.
4. **Literature use:** Lacroix et al. (2023) — the paper's most heavily reused single citation across the Discussion (appearing in §5.2, §5.3 twice, and here), each time for a genuinely distinct comparative purpose (timing concordance for the 3-month critical window; timing discordance for acceleration onset), showing a citation being used for both concordant and discordant purposes across different paragraphs, a variant of the B2-type dual-use pattern from the original 9-paper file, but spread across the Discussion rather than confined to one paragraph.
5. **Certainty level:** The InSAR-earlier-detection claim is stated with a light hedge ("suggest that") appropriate for a genuinely novel finding the paper is the first to report; the rainfall-insufficiency claim is stated with a light hedge ("suggesting that") grounded directly in the paper's own comparative year-to-year data, an appropriate basis for moderate confidence.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** The InSAR-vs-optical timing discordance is a central, load-bearing discordance for this paper's overall contribution claim (its methods detect earlier precursors) rather than a peripheral one — handled directly, without hedging away the magnitude of the difference (2 months earlier).
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Implicit — directly supports the paper's central early-warning-lead-time claim (its own method detects the critical transition earlier than a prior, independent, optical-based method).
8. **Limitations:** None new in this paragraph.
9. **Role:** Core interpretive, central discordance (of a favorable/novelty-supporting kind) + rival-hypothesis elimination (rainfall-alone) — a distinctive combination not seen elsewhere in the corpus in one paragraph.

#### §5.4.2 — Paragraph 3
1. **Function:** Core interpretive — a within-study quantitative kinematic comparison across the paper's own four identified activity phases (using inverse-velocity plots), distinguishing the final, fatal phase from the earlier, self-arresting phases via a specific, citable diagnostic criterion.
2. **Finding it starts from:** Fig. 7's inverse-velocity (1/v) comparison of Phases 1, 2, and 4.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct description of each phase's inverse-velocity trajectory, then an explicit diagnostic distinction: "Phases 1 and 2 show an abrupt initial drop followed by flattening at values above zero, **consistent with** asymptotic decay and indicating the attainment of temporary steady states following transient accelerations (Carlà et al., 2017). **In contrast**, phase 4 declines more gradually but approaches near-zero inverse velocity within ~3 months, **signalling** sustained acceleration towards failure rather than stabilisation."
4. **Literature use:** Carlà et al. (2017) supports the general inverse-velocity/asymptotic-decay diagnostic framework being applied to distinguish stabilizing vs. failure-bound kinematic behavior — an A3-type application of an established diagnostic method to this paper's own new data, used here to interpret (not merely describe) the phase comparison.
5. **Certainty level:** The phases 1-2 steady-state interpretation is stated with the confidence of an established, literature-validated diagnostic ("consistent with"); the phase-4 failure-bound interpretation is stated slightly more assertively ("signalling") but grounded in the same established framework, appropriate confidence for a within-study comparison using a validated method.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** The explicit "in contrast" framing directly distinguishes the failure-bound phase from the non-failure-bound phases using the same diagnostic tool — an internal (not external-literature) discordance comparison, structurally similar to Zhu et al.'s rural/urban precipitation-correlation contrast in the original 9-paper file, but here comparing time-phases of one case rather than two spatial subgroups.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Closing sentence draws a direct process-level interpretation: "This pattern suggests the slope did not fully recover stability between acceleration episodes, supporting the interpretation of progressive internal degradation and gradual accumulation of strain" — ties the quantitative kinematic comparison back to the paper's overarching internal-damage-accumulation narrative.
8. **Limitations:** None new.
9. **Role:** Core interpretive, internal discordance/contrast handling using an externally validated diagnostic method.

#### §5.4.2 — Paragraph 4
1. **Function:** Rival-hypothesis elimination (external forcing insufficiency, restated with a stronger direct claim) plus a mechanistic close naming the specific microscale process (microcrack formation/coalescence) inferred to underlie the macroscale kinematic pattern.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The cumulative Phase 1-4 kinematic and forcing-timing evidence.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** A direct, less-hedged elimination statement than earlier in the Discussion: "While external forcing factors, such as rainfall and seismic activity, modulated the timing and magnitude of these accelerations, **they cannot alone explain** the observed behaviour." This is supported by two specific counter-examples restated compactly (comparable 2019-2020 rainfall not triggering failure; acceleration continuing through the 2018 dry season). The paragraph then proposes the specific microscale mechanism: "The 1/v plots highlight the internal process of material degradation, including microcrack formation and coalescence, leading to the development of a shear surface by March 2020."
4. **Literature use:** None new in this paragraph — self-contained synthesis of the paper's own preceding evidence.
5. **Certainty level:** The elimination claim ("cannot alone explain") is stated with notably higher confidence than the earlier, more tentative "may be more critical than" hedge in §5.4.1 — reflecting that by this point in the Discussion, the paper has accumulated multiple independent lines of its own evidence (three separate instances across different time periods) for the same conclusion, which appropriately licenses a stronger, less-hedged statement than a single instance would.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** N/A (synthesis of already-established internal evidence).
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Direct, methodological: "By observing these behaviours, we demonstrate how dimensionless parameters from the wrapped phase can capture meaningful kinematic evolution, offering valuable insight into early destabilisation mechanisms" — a methodological-contribution implication, not a site-management policy recommendation (consistent with this paper's technical/scientific research question).
8. **Limitations:** None new.
9. **Role:** Core interpretive, cumulative rival-hypothesis elimination + microscale mechanistic close + methodological implication.

#### §5.4.2 — Paragraph 5
1. **Function:** Explicit, locally placed limitation on the paper's own central diagnostic tool (the inverse-velocity/1/v analysis), immediately followed by a scope-clarifying statement of what the tool is and is not meant to do.
2. **Finding it starts from:** Phase 4's inverse-velocity trend being truncated ~3 months before failure due to increasing measurement noise.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct limitation statement: "phase 4 is truncated roughly 3 months prior to failure due to increasing noise in the dimensionless measure, **preventing observation of** the full 1/v trend as the landslide approaches collapse." Immediately paired with an explicit scope-correction: "These inverse velocity plots are therefore **not intended to predict** failure timing but to illustrate the changing kinematic behaviour across acceleration phases" — a precise statement of what the evidence can and cannot support, directly analogous to the C3-type two-tier can-show/cannot-show bounding pattern from the original 9-paper file (e.g., Chounna Yemele's GRACE-data bounding).
4. **Literature use:** None.
5. **Certainty level:** Stated plainly and directly, not hedged as uncertain about whether the limitation exists — a precise, quantitatively scoped (3-month truncation window) limitation.
6. **Concordant/discordant:** N/A.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** The scope-correction functions as a pre-emptive limitation on how strongly the paper's own central early-warning framing (elsewhere in the Discussion) should be read — an important internal check against overclaiming operational failure-prediction capability from a method explicitly stated to not predict failure timing.
8. **Limitations bounding:** A clean, textbook instance of the C3 two-tier bounding pattern — precisely stating what the 1/v analysis demonstrates (kinematic behavior change) versus what it does not (failure-timing prediction).
9. **Role:** Limitation, locally placed immediately after the specific claim (Phase 4's 1/v trend) it qualifies, with an explicit can-show/cannot-show scope statement.

#### §5.4.2 — Paragraph 6 (river erosion)
1. **Function:** Rival-hypothesis elimination — tests and rejects river-toe erosion as a triggering mechanism using an independent optical dataset, with an explicit data-limitation caveat stated before the conclusion.
2. **Finding it starts from:** PlanetScope-derived riverbank-position and river-width measurements across five wet seasons (2016-2020).
3. **Mechanism of the move:** An explicit limitation-then-conclusion structure: "Despite potential image resolution limitations, our observations reveal no clear trend of significant erosion toward the left bank or landslide toe" — the limitation is named before the null finding is asserted, not after, ensuring the reader weighs the caveat while reading the conclusion rather than after. A partial, hedged secondary observation follows: "The right bank shows a slight tendency to move away, **possibly due to** the landslide toe pushing the riverbed, which **may increase** erosion on that side" — doubly hedged, explicitly speculative.
4. **Literature use:** None in this paragraph — self-contained analysis of the paper's own PlanetScope dataset.
5. **Certainty level:** The main null finding (no significant erosion trend) is stated with the caveat pre-registered (image-resolution limitation named first); the secondary toe-pushing-riverbed observation is doubly hedged ("possibly," "may increase") as clearly the weaker, more speculative sub-claim.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** A null result (no erosion trend) is treated as informative — directly supporting the paper's own concluding statement that the landslide was driven by long-term internal progressive failure, not an external river-undercutting trigger — a clean instance of a null result functioning as positive evidence for the paper's preferred explanation, precisely the "null result read as informative confirmation of a modeling assumption" pattern (A2, extended) documented in the original 9-paper file for Rodrigues do Amaral.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Direct closing statement: "These findings suggest that the landslide was not triggered by high river erosion, but rather by a long-term process of progressive failure" — the paragraph's own explicit synthesis, tying the eliminated rival hypothesis (river erosion) back to the paper's central preferred explanation (progressive internal failure), closing §5.4 as a whole.
8. **Limitations bounding:** The image-resolution caveat is placed at the point of maximum relevance (immediately before the finding it could weaken), a locally-placed limitation in the A4a tradition.
9. **Role:** Core interpretive, rival-hypothesis elimination (river erosion) using an independent dataset, closing §5.4 with a direct tie-back to the paper's central thesis.

#### §5.5 "Complementarity" — Paragraph 1
1. **Function:** Synthesis paragraph — consolidates the two central markers' relative timing (activity index detecting acceleration onset in January 2020; coherence detecting critical instability in March 2020) into a single narrative, validates the overall early-detection framing against the same external comparator used earlier, and explicitly differentiates the paper's own methodological approach from two named alternative approaches in the literature.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The cumulative timing evidence from §5.3 and §5.4.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct synthesis: "We retrospectively identify the beginning of a final acceleration already in January 2020..., transitioning into a critical and irreversible instability in March 2020..., **indicating that** proactive monitoring could have commenced at least at this critical stage." Concordance validation follows: "This finding **echoes** Jacquemart and Tiampo's (2021) observation of a coherence ratio drop 5 months before the failure of the Mudcreek landslide, emphasising its potential as early indicator." The paragraph then adds an explicit methodological contrast with two named alternative approaches: "They also noted that an approach based on time series generation underestimated displacements. **In contrast**, (Handwerger et al., 2019) used a preprocessing step for signal removal before unwrapping.... Our method... offers a more streamlined alternative... **less time-consuming**."
4. **Literature use:** Jacquemart and Tiampo (2021) re-cited a third time (after §5.3's two uses) for a general concordance validation of the early-indicator concept; Handwerger et al. (2019) is introduced specifically to contrast methodological complexity (a preprocessing-heavy alternative approach) against this paper's own simpler wrapped-phase method — a comparative-methodology literature use distinct from either concordance-of-findings or attributed-mechanism uses seen elsewhere, functioning to position the paper's own contribution's practical/operational advantage (simplicity, speed) rather than its numeric accuracy.
5. **Certainty level:** The synthesis statement and methodological-comparison claims are stated fairly directly, appropriate for describing the paper's own demonstrated method and its comparison to previously published alternatives.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** Fully concordant with Jacquemart and Tiampo (2021) at the level of "coherence-based markers provide early warning value"; implicitly favorable-comparative (not directly discordant) with Handwerger et al. (2019) on methodological complexity/efficiency grounds.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Direct, blending methodological contribution ("our method... is less time-consuming") and practical relevance (early monitoring "could have commenced" at the critical stage) — both implication types present in the same paragraph.
8. **Limitations:** None new.
9. **Role:** Closing synthesis (opening the final Discussion subsection), concordance-validating + methodological-contribution positioning.

#### §5.5 — Paragraph 2 (closing)
1. **Function:** Closing paragraph for the entire Discussion — states the current method's practical scope limitation (requires prior knowledge of landslide location), proposes future extensions (automated large-scale detection via machine learning, cross-referencing named prior AI work), and closes with a complementarity argument positioning the paper's method relative to a distinct, independently emerging monitoring technology (optical feature tracking).
2. **Finding it starts from:** The cumulative demonstrated value of the coherence and wrapped-phase markers at this single, already-known landslide site.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** An explicit scope-limitation-then-research-agenda structure: "determining whether they can be reliably detected **without prior knowledge of** landslide locations **will require** large-scale analysis across diverse terrains... Evaluating the generalisability of this methodology across different landslide types and settings is a key goal for future work." A specific technological extension is then proposed and grounded in a cited precedent: "automated detection of these features using machine learning techniques could significantly enhance landslide monitoring. Approaches such as that proposed by Chen et al. (2022) demonstrate the feasibility of applying deep semantic segmentation to recognise active landslides from InSAR." The paragraph then closes with a complementarity argument against a different, independently emerging method: "the approach proposed here could be highly complementary to recent optical monitoring methods... Van Wyk de Vries et al. (2024) demonstrated that automated Sentinel-2 feature tracking can identify surface deformation for landslides attaining larger velocities than InSAR can detect... In contrast, InSAR-based coherence and wrapped-phase indicators may be more sensitive to subtler or intermittent deformation."
4. **Literature use:** Chen et al. (2022) supports the machine-learning-automation extension as a feasible, precedented direction (not yet applied by this paper itself) — a forward-looking, precedent-citing use; Van Wyk de Vries et al. (2024) is used for an explicit capability-boundary comparison (optical methods detect faster/larger deformation; InSAR-based markers detect subtler/intermittent deformation) — a comparative-scope literature use that positions the paper's contribution as complementary to, not competing with, a different sensing modality, rather than claiming superiority.
5. **Certainty level:** The generalizability limitation is stated plainly as an open, unresolved question ("will require... is a key goal for future work"), not overclaimed as already demonstrated; the machine-learning extension and optical-complementarity claims are both explicitly framed as forward-looking possibilities ("could significantly enhance," "could be highly complementary"), appropriately hedged since neither is implemented or tested within this paper.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** N/A — a forward-looking synthesis, not a comparison of findings.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** The paragraph is itself almost entirely composed of implications — a research-agenda-level implication (generalizability testing, ML automation) rather than an immediate practical/policy recommendation, appropriate given the paper's single-case, method-demonstration design (no decision-relevant quantity, such as an extraction rate or design elevation, is at stake here — the "actionable" content is a proposed future research/engineering direction, not an action a stakeholder can take today).
8. **Limitations bounding:** The core, paper-level limitation (demonstrated on one known site; generalizability to unknown-location detection untested) is named directly in the Discussion's final substantive paragraph, with no subsequent affirmative synthesis after it — the Discussion's very last paragraph is this one, and it ends on the optical-complementarity forward-looking framing rather than returning to a summary statement of what was demonstrated.
9. **Role:** Closing — a genuine hybrid of forward-research-call (closing-move type b, per the original 9-paper file's Step 4 typology) and reassertion-of-contribution-via-complementarity (closing-move type a), rather than a pure limitation-terminal close (type c) or a pure affirmative synthesis; ends the Discussion on an outward-looking, field-positioning note immediately before the Conclusions section begins.

**Note on this paper's structural character:** This Discussion is the most citation-dense, mechanism-focused single-case-study Discussion in the 15-paper set — nearly every paragraph deploys at least one specific named literature comparator, and several (Jacquemart and Tiampo 2021; Lacroix et al. 2023; Agliardi et al. 2020) are reused multiple times across different paragraphs for genuinely distinct purposes (concordance, discordance, methodological contrast, mechanism-borrowing) — an unusually extensive within-paper instance of the B2-type "dual/multi-use of one citation" pattern from the original 9-paper file, here spread across a whole Discussion rather than confined to one paragraph. Rival-hypothesis elimination (C1-type) is exceptionally frequent and well-developed (land-cover vs. deformation as coherence-loss cause; seismicity-alone vs. combined seismicity-rainfall; rainfall-alone vs. accumulated damage; river erosion vs. progressive internal failure) — four distinct instances in this single paper, more than any other single paper in the corpus, strongly reinforcing C1 as a real, recurring move once enough process-attribution-heavy papers are included. The paper's implications are consistently methodological/research-agenda-level (A5's "not a decision-relevant, actionable-by-a-stakeholder quantity" qualification applies cleanly here, similar to Strozzi et al., Paper 10) rather than policy-prescriptive, appropriate for a single-site method-demonstration paper whose "stakeholder" is the landslide-monitoring research community itself.
## PAPER 13: Cavalié, Cappa & Pinel-Puysségur 2023 — Nice Côte d'Azur Airport coastal subsidence

**Citation:** Cavalié, O., Cappa, F., and Pinel-Puysségur, B. (2023). *Three decades of coastal subsidence in the slow-moving Nice Côte d'Azur Airport area (France) revealed by InSAR (interferometric synthetic-aperture radar): insights into the deformation mechanism.* Nat. Hazards Earth Syst. Sci., 23, 3235– (2023). URL: https://nhess.copernicus.org/articles/23/3235/2023/

**Status: SUCCESS.** Full research article confirmed. Complete verbatim text recovered for the entire merged "Discussion and conclusion" section (4 paragraphs), citations intact, via a single successful WebFetch pass. **No standalone Discussion section — merged as "Discussion and conclusion,"** directly following `5 Creep modelling`, with no separate Conclusions section afterward (this is a structural variant not seen in the original 9-paper file: Shahi and Li both merge Results-and-discussion but still have a SEPARATE, later Conclusions section; here Discussion and Conclusion are merged into ONE terminal section with nothing following it except the back-matter — data availability, author contributions, etc.).

### STEP 1 — Foundations
- **Research question:** What is the long-term (multi-decade) subsidence history of the Nice Côte d'Azur Airport platform (built on reclaimed, artificially loaded land over unconsolidated Holocene river-delta sediment), and what rheological/mechanical mechanism governs its currently decelerating but still-active deformation?
- **Objective:** Extend a prior short-window (2003-2011, Envisat-only) InSAR subsidence measurement at this site to a 28-year, three-satellite-generation record (ERS 1992-2001, Envisat 2003-2011, Sentinel-1 2014-2020), and fit an extended viscoelastic Burgers creep model (Maxwell + Kelvin bodies) to the resulting time series to extract physically interpretable sediment-mechanical parameters and characterize which stage of the creep process the deformation is currently in.
- **Study design:** Empirical single-site InSAR time-series reconstruction (NSBAS/small baseline subset processing) plus analytical rheological model-fitting (not an experiment) — three sequential SAR missions stitched into one continuous 28-year displacement record; a Burgers creep model (primary + secondary creep phases) fit to the time series via chi-squared minimization to retrieve applied stress, Maxwell elastic modulus, and Kelvin viscosity parameters.
- **Population/sample:** Not a human population — a single infrastructure site (Nice Côte d'Azur Airport platform, Var River delta, France), built on reclaimed land over >100 m of Holocene alluvium atop Pliocene conglomerate, Early Pleistocene marls, and Jurassic limestone; adjacent to a narrow continental shelf that drops sharply to −2500 m.
- **Key findings the Discussion/conclusion must interpret:**
  - Subsidence rate has decelerated across all three observation windows: ~16 mm/yr (1992-2001) → 9.5 mm/yr (2003-2011) → 8 mm/yr (2014-2020), with velocity uncertainty ~0.3 mm/yr.
  - Velocity drops sharply to zero over the consolidated conglomerate — a spatially precise, geology-matched boundary condition.
  - The best-fit Burgers creep model (chi-squared = 0.18957) indicates the deformation is best explained by primary-and-secondary creep phases, and is still in the primary stage — meaning the future trajectory (further deceleration, stabilization, or eventual acceleration toward failure) is not determined by the fit alone.
  - The extended 28-year record reveals a transient, non-linear deceleration pattern that a shorter (2003-2011-only) record could not resolve, directly improving on a named prior study by the same lead author (Cavalié et al., 2015).

### STEP 2 — Discussion (merged "Discussion and conclusion") paragraph-by-paragraph

#### Paragraph 1
1. **Function:** Opening/orienting — situates the paper's specific site within the general global phenomenon of coastal subsidence (both anthropogenic/groundwater-driven and natural/delta-compaction-driven), then names the specific compounding mechanism at this site (natural compaction PLUS artificial loading) before any new interpretation of this paper's own numeric results begins.
2. **Finding it starts from:** No new numeric finding yet — general framing plus a direct reference to the site's known compounding-mechanism history.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** General-to-specific funnel: broad coastal-subsidence framing (groundwater-pumping-driven cases like Jakarta, contrasted with natural delta-compaction cases like the Nile, Mississippi, Po), then narrows directly to this site's own compounding case: "we observe the interaction between the natural compaction of the unconsolidated Holocene sediments... with the construction of artificial infrastructure (NCA) that brought an additional superficial load... This extra load leads to a drastic amplification of the subsidence rate as observed in Cavalié et al. (2015)." A second compounding hazard factor is then named: the reclaimed land sits atop a narrow continental shelf that drops sharply to −2500 m, amplifying the practical danger of the subsidence (implying slope-failure risk, developed further in Paragraph 3).
4. **Literature use:** Cavalié et al. (2015) — the authors' own prior, shorter-window study on the same site — cited here to establish the load-amplification mechanism as an already-established finding this paper builds on, not a new discovery of this paper; a self-citation used to position the present paper as an extension of established local knowledge rather than a new claim.
5. **Certainty level:** The general subsidence-driver taxonomy (groundwater-pumping cases vs. natural-compaction cases) is stated as established background fact; the site-specific load-amplification mechanism is also stated directly, appropriately since it is attributed to a specific prior study (Cavalié et al. 2015) that already demonstrated it, not asserted as newly proven by this paper alone.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** N/A.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Closing sentence states the paper's motivating rationale directly: "Understanding the mechanism and thus the evolution of sediment compaction is essential to evaluate the danger caused by the coastal subsidence" — a direct bridge from the framing to why the paper's own extended-record and creep-modeling approach matters.
8. **Limitations:** None yet.
9. **Role:** Opening/orienting, general-to-specific funnel, self-citation establishing continuity with prior work rather than introducing new claims.

#### Paragraph 2
1. **Function:** Core interpretive — reports the paper's own central methodological/empirical contribution (extending the observation window from 8 years to 28 years) and explicitly argues WHY this extension mattered by naming precisely what the shorter prior study could not resolve, then reports the creep-model fitting result as validating a specific, physically expected deformation pattern.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The 28-year, tripled-length observation window (vs. the prior study's 2003-2011 Envisat-only window) and the resulting non-linear, decelerating subsidence time series.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct comparative-methodology statement: "the previous study (Cavalié et al., 2015) measured the airport platform subsidence using only the Envisat data that span the 2003–2011 period. This relatively short period of observation **impeded the accurate detection of** non-linearity in the surface displacement and its change rate." This is a precise, mechanism-level statement of exactly what limitation the longer record fixes (detectability of non-linearity), not a vague "more data is better" claim. The paper then states the creep model's validating result: "The data are properly explained by the phases of primary and secondary creep... The best-fit solution allows for retrieving reasonable mechanical values" — "reasonable" here functions as an implicit concordance claim (the retrieved parameters are physically plausible for this sediment type), though without an explicit external numeric comparator cited in this specific sentence.
4. **Literature use:** Cavalié et al. (2015) re-cited, now specifically to name what its shorter window could NOT show — a direct comparative-methodology use of the authors' own prior work, positioning the present paper's specific value-add precisely (temporal-resolution improvement enabling non-linearity detection) rather than a generic "we did more/better."
5. **Certainty level:** The record-extension benefit is stated as directly demonstrated fact (a straightforward comparison of what each dataset's time span could/could not resolve); the creep-model fit's "reasonable" mechanical values is a moderate-confidence claim, appropriately calibrated since "reasonable" is a qualitative, not quantitatively benchmarked, validation within this paragraph.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** Implicit concordance — the decelerating pattern is explicitly noted as "expected for ground layer compaction," meaning the paper's own finding matches the general physical expectation for this class of process, stated directly and without a specific external citation attached to that exact clause.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Closing sentence functions as the paper's central methodological-contribution implication: "Our study thus proves that the long-term InSAR data can improve our understanding of the surface processes and the subsurface material properties" — a fairly direct, unhedged claim ("proves") for a methodological/scientific-contribution implication, notably less hedged than most implication statements elsewhere in the corpus, though appropriately scoped to a methodological (not policy) claim.
8. **Limitations:** None new in this paragraph — the "impeded accurate detection" phrase functions retrospectively as a named limitation of the PRIOR study, not of this paper's own work.
9. **Role:** Core interpretive, comparative-methodology (against the authors' own prior narrower-scope study) + implicit concordance (matches physical expectation) + direct methodological-contribution implication.

#### Paragraph 3
1. **Function:** Core interpretive plus extensive limitations/uncertainty-bounding — states the central forward-looking uncertainty (future trajectory of the still-active creep process is undetermined), then works through a chain of physically plausible but explicitly unconfirmed contributing/triggering factors, and closes with two distinct forward-research recommendations (laboratory experiments; field geotechnical/hydrogeological/seismic data collection).
2. **Finding it starts from:** The creep model's finding that deformation is still in the "primary stage" of the creep process — an explicitly open-ended state, not a stabilizing or terminal one.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** A direct, unhedged statement of genuine uncertainty as the paragraph's organizing premise: "our results show that the compaction of the sediment is still active and its future evolution is uncertain and still at stake." This licenses a chain of explicitly conditional statements: "**if** compaction bands are developing under the airport platform, creep processes **could potentially** lead accumulated material damage to failure" — an if-then, explicitly hedged causal chain, not a claim that this is happening. A list of possible (not confirmed) contributing stress factors follows, each stated with a hedge appropriate to its own evidentiary basis: "The clay sediments **can creep at varying rates due to** different stress factors, such as airport platform loading and potential fluid pressure increases from precipitation. Earthquake shaking in this seismic area **could also accelerate** creep rates... When subjected to high deviatoric load, significant creep **may occur**... an increase in fluid pressure **can reduce** effective stress and **expedite** sediment creeping to slope failure." The paragraph explicitly names what IS established with confidence, separately from what is not: "Presently, it is clear that the airport platform is slowly creeping, and multiple triggering processes are possible" — directly distinguishing a confirmed observation (creep is occurring) from an unconfirmed, multi-candidate causal question (which trigger, if any, will drive future acceleration).
4. **Literature use:** None new in this paragraph — this is a physically reasoned, own-domain-knowledge chain of hypotheses, not literature-attributed.
5. **Certainty level:** Explicitly and carefully multi-tiered within a single paragraph: the ongoing-creep observation is stated as established fact ("it is clear"); every downstream consequence (compaction-band development, failure risk, specific trigger mechanisms) is explicitly and repeatedly hedged ("if," "could potentially," "can," "could also," "may occur") — one of the most systematically and consistently hedged single paragraphs across the 15-paper corpus, appropriate given the paper's own creep model cannot, by its own stated logic (still in the primary stage), determine whether the process will stabilize or progress toward failure.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** N/A — this paragraph is forward-looking uncertainty characterization, not a comparison against prior findings.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Two distinct, explicitly scoped recommendations follow directly from the uncertainty: (a) "conducting controlled laboratory experiments under varying disturbance amplitudes and frequencies would be highly beneficial" (a specific, mechanistic research recommendation, tied directly to isolating "the specific influence of different factors on the creep deformation at each stage"); (b) "it would be beneficial to gather high-resolution geotechnical, hydrogeological, and seismic data across the Var River delta platform and slope. Combined with geomechanical modelling, such data could help to validate the assumptions regarding the slope's stability." Both recommendations are research-agenda-level (what data/experiments are needed), not action-now policy prescriptions (e.g., no explicit call to halt operations, reinforce the platform, or relocate infrastructure) — an appropriately scoped implication given the paper's own explicit statement that the current data cannot resolve the key uncertainty (future trajectory).
8. **Limitations bounding:** This paragraph is functionally almost entirely a limitations/uncertainty-bounding passage — the central, unresolved uncertainty (can the current data and model determine future trajectory? No, explicitly) governs the entire paragraph's hedged structure, making it one of the clearest examples in the corpus of a paper stating precisely what it does NOT yet know rather than either overclaiming resolution or omitting the open question. A specific counterfactual scope-clarification closes the paragraph: "even if the airport platform were absent, the slope would likely still experience creep due to the clay layer, although at a much slower rate" — a precise statement disentangling the natural background process from the anthropogenic-loading amplification, preventing the reader from over-attributing all observed creep to the airport construction alone.
9. **Role:** Core interpretive + extensive, carefully tiered uncertainty-bounding + dual research-agenda-level (not policy-level) implications — the paper's central "here is what we don't yet know and what would help find out" passage.

#### Paragraph 4 (closing)
1. **Function:** Closing paragraph for the entire merged Discussion-and-conclusion — connects the site-specific finding to a broader, global climate-change/sea-level-rise framing, and closes with a general practical-relevance implication addressed to a named category of stakeholder (managers and policymakers), rather than a site-specific action recommendation or a further limitation.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The cumulative findings of the paper (28-year subsidence record, creep-model characterization, unresolved future-trajectory uncertainty).
3. **Mechanism of the move:** A broadening, contextualizing statement: "In an era in which climate change and sea level rise pose unprecedented threats to coastal ecosystems and urbanization (Shirzaei and Bürgmann, 2018; Shirzaei et al., 2021), the long-term observations of ground motion from space are essential to monitor the stability of coastal environments and will inform managers and policymakers in identifying zones with exposure to hazards."
4. **Literature use:** Shirzaei and Bürgmann (2018), Shirzaei et al. (2021) cited to support the general climate-change/sea-level-rise threat-context framing — a broadening, motivational citation use (establishing why the paper's general topic matters at a global scale) rather than a site-specific concordance/discordance validation of this paper's own numeric findings.
5. **Certainty level:** Stated as a direct, general, largely unhedged practical-relevance claim ("is essential to monitor," "will inform") — appropriate register for a closing paragraph making a general case for the value of the paper's methodological approach (long-term InSAR monitoring) rather than a specific quantitative claim requiring hedging.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** N/A.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** This paragraph IS the paper's practical/policy implication payload — but notably general and stakeholder-category-level ("managers and policymakers," "identifying zones with exposure to hazards") rather than site-specific or quantitatively prescriptive (contrast with, e.g., Nguyen [Ca Mau]'s itemized, driver-by-driver recommendation list in the original 9-paper file, or Zhu et al.'s specific "coal extraction must be carried out very carefully" safety recommendation) — an appropriately scoped implication given that Paragraph 3 has just explicitly stated the future-trajectory question remains unresolved; the paper does not overreach into site-specific prescriptive recommendations (e.g., "the airport should do X") that its own stated uncertainty could not support.
8. **Limitations:** None new — this paragraph does not add a limitation, it closes on an affirmative, generalized practical-relevance statement.
9. **Role:** Closing — a genuine affirmative-synthesis-and-broadening close (closing-move type (a) in the original 9-paper file's Step 4 typology: reassertion of the paper's broader method/contribution, generalized to a wider policy-relevant framing), notable because this closing paragraph is ALSO, structurally, the paper's formal Conclusion (since Discussion and Conclusion are merged into one terminal section with no further section following it) — meaning this single paragraph must simultaneously perform the closing-Discussion-move function AND the formal-Conclusion function that in every other paper in the corpus are handled by two separate sections. This is a genuinely novel structural variant relative to both the original 9-paper file (where merged-section papers, Shahi and Li, still had a SEPARATE later Conclusions section) and the other 5 new papers in this batch.

**Note on this paper's structural character:** This is the first paper across all 15 in the corpus with NO separate Conclusions section at all — Discussion and Conclusion are fully fused into one terminal section, immediately followed only by back-matter (data availability, author contributions, etc.). This is structurally distinct from Shahi et al. and Li et al. (the two merged-Results-and-Discussion papers in the original 9-paper file), both of which still had their own later, separate Conclusions section. This matters directly for the Step 3 cross-paper B3/merged-structure pattern and for testing where "the last word" of the Discussion-equivalent content falls: here, Paragraph 4's affirmative, generalized policy-relevance closing genuinely IS the paper's very last analytical content, with nothing after it but formal back-matter — a clean, unambiguous instance of closing-move type (a), unlike some ambiguous cases in the 9-paper file where the boundary between "Discussion's last paragraph" and "the separate Conclusion section" required interpretation. A5 (practical/policy implication) is present but generalized/stakeholder-category-level rather than site-specific-prescriptive — worth flagging precisely because the underlying finding (future trajectory of creep is explicitly unresolved) could not support a more specific, actionable recommendation, an instance directly supporting the A5 qualification already established in the original file (implications calibrate to the finding's certainty and actionability, not always maximally prescriptive).
## PAPER 14: Barthelemy et al. 2024 — Drought-index adjustment for clay-shrinkage subsidence, France

**Citation:** Barthelemy, S., Bonan, B., Calvet, J.-C., Grandjean, G., Moncoulon, D., Kapsambelis, D., and Bernardie, S. (2024). *A new approach for drought index adjustment to clay-shrinkage-induced subsidence over France: advantages of the interactive leaf area index.* Nat. Hazards Earth Syst. Sci., 24, 999– (2024). URL: https://nhess.copernicus.org/articles/24/999/2024/

**Status: SUCCESS.** Full research article confirmed. Complete, essentially fully verbatim Discussion text recovered for all five subsections (4.1-4.5) across four WebFetch calls, citations intact throughout, including a notable subsection organized explicitly as a posed-and-answered question ("Is the optimal drought magnitude a reliable proxy for subsidence damage?" / "What are the possible applications... in and outside of France?"). Standalone Discussion (`4 Discussion`), separate from `3 Results`, followed by `5 Conclusion`.

### STEP 1 — Foundations
- **Research question:** (a) Which soil depth layer's soil-wetness-index (SWI) drought signal best predicts clay-shrinkage subsidence damage? (b) Does allowing the land-surface model's leaf area index (LAI) to respond interactively to soil moisture/temperature/radiation (rather than using a fixed climatological LAI cycle) improve that prediction, and if so, why?
- **Objective:** Develop and validate a new annual "drought magnitude" index (summed dry-anomaly of daily SWI) derived from the ISBA land-surface model (within SURFEX), tested across 10 soil layers, two LAI configurations (LAI_clim vs. LAI_model), and multiple percentile drought thresholds, against 19 years (2000-2018) of French national insurance subsidence-claim records (CCR/CatNat database) for 26 municipalities.
- **Study design:** Empirical model-development and validation study — ISBA land-surface simulations (10-layer diffusive soil-moisture scheme, 8 km grid) run in two configurations (prescribed vs. interactive LAI); 1200 candidate index configurations (layer x LAI-configuration x threshold x index-type: duration/severity/magnitude) evaluated via Kendall's tau correlation against municipality-level, population-normalized insurance-claim counts; five 4-municipality calibration subsets plus a separate 6-municipality validation set to guard against overfitting.
- **Population/sample:** Not a human population directly — 26 French municipalities across different climate zones, housing stock in moderate-to-high clay-shrinkage-hazard zones; insurance claims aggregated annually per municipality, 2000-2018, from the CCR (Caisse Centrale de Réassurance).
- **Key findings the Discussion must interpret:**
  - Best-performing configuration: magnitude index (not duration or severity), based on SWI of the 0.8-1.0 m soil layer (layer 8), from the LAI_model (interactive LAI) simulation, using extreme (low, 1st-5th percentile) drought thresholds — average Kendall tau = 0.42 (calibration set, p <= 0.001), validation-set tau = 0.23 (p < 0.001).
  - LAI_model outperforms LAI_clim, and deeper soil layers outperform shallow ones — two separate methodological choices both shown to matter, requiring separate mechanistic explanations.
  - The index correctly flags major documented drought years (2003, 2018) at more than half the study sites, but shows genuine discrepancies in both directions: drought years with no recorded damage, and damage recorded in non-drought years.
  - Multiple distinct, named sources of uncertainty are explicitly catalogued (insurance-data representativeness, a circularity between the index and the French CatNat compensation-decree meteorological criterion, temporal/spatial resolution mismatches, neglect of multi-year cumulative drought effects, coarse clay-exposure zoning, and unmeasured local/building-level factors).

### STEP 2 — Discussion paragraph-by-paragraph

#### §4.1 "How does the drought magnitude compare to existing drought indices?" — Paragraph 1
1. **Function:** Opening/orienting — justifies the paper's own foundational design choice (an LSM-derived, soil-moisture-based index rather than a purely meteorological one) by direct appeal to the physical nature of the phenomenon under study.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The paper's own choice to build the drought index from ISBA-simulated soil wetness rather than a meteorological drought index.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct physical-reasoning chain, unhedged: "clay shrinkage is a physical response of soils to drying. Therefore, the most relevant way to monitor it is to focus on soil moisture variations. Meteorological variables alone are not able to provide a sufficient description of the hydric state of soils due to the multiple interactions between atmosphere, soil, and vegetation." This is immediately grounded in a specific prior comparative finding: "hence the difference between the SPI and SSWI noted by Soubeyroux et al. (2012) and Charpentier et al. (2022)."
4. **Literature use:** Soubeyroux et al. (2012), Charpentier et al. (2022) cited to support the general claim that meteorological indices (SPI) and soil-moisture-based indices (SSWI) diverge meaningfully — a concordant, foundational-justification use; WMO and GWP (2016) cited to support the general design-space claim that soil-moisture assessment spans a spectrum from simple water-balance models to complex LSMs.
5. **Certainty level:** Stated as established domain knowledge, not hedged — appropriate since this is a well-supported methodological premise, not a novel claim of this paper.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** N/A.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Implicit — justifies the entire paper's modeling approach before any of its own new results are discussed.
8. **Limitations:** None yet.
9. **Role:** Opening/orienting, methodological-justification.

#### §4.1 — Paragraph 2
1. **Function:** Core interpretive — explicit discordance/non-comparability statement against the closest prior methodological precedent (a directly analogous SWI-based magnitude index by a named prior study), explaining precisely why this paper could not adopt that precedent's method, then stating what element was nonetheless retained.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The paper's own magnitude-index calculation method, compared against Vidal et al. (2010)'s SSWI-based magnitude index.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct discordance framing: "Although both are based on an ISBA-modeled SWI, our approach has a **major difference from** the one developed by Vidal et al. (2010)." The reason for the divergence is then explained precisely, not as a design preference but as a data-availability constraint: "This method was **not applicable to our work because** the insurance data lead us to focus on the years 2000–2018, a period which is too short for a proper distributional analysis of the monthly SWI." The paragraph closes by naming what was preserved despite the divergence: "the standardization step is still included in the index calculation, since the thresholds are based on SWI percentiles" — a partial-concordance closing that prevents the divergence from being overstated as total.
4. **Literature use:** Vidal et al. (2010) — the single closest prior methodological precedent, used for an explicit, reasoned discordance (not a vague "different from prior work" statement, but a precise data-length constraint that forced the divergence) — structurally similar to the B2-type dual concordant/discordant single-citation use seen in Shahi et al. (original 9-paper file) and Dini et al. (Paper 12, this batch), here used once but for a clean, single, well-reasoned discordance rather than a split concordant/discordant treatment.
5. **Certainty level:** Stated as a plain factual/methodological constraint (record length), not hedged as uncertain about whether the constraint applies.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** A clear, non-defensive discordance-explanation: the paper does not claim its approach is superior to Vidal et al. (2010), only that it was the necessary adaptation given a shorter usable record — an honest, constraint-driven justification rather than a claim of methodological improvement.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Implicit — establishes the index's own internal validity basis (percentile standardization) before the Discussion moves to interpreting why specific configuration choices (LAI, soil layer) performed best.
8. **Limitations:** The short record length (2000-2018, forced by insurance-data availability) is named here as the direct cause of a methodological compromise — a limitation stated as an explanation for a design choice rather than as an open caveat on the findings.
9. **Role:** Core interpretive, methodological discordance-explanation (constraint-driven, not comparative-superiority-driven).

#### §4.2 "Why is an interactive LAI needed?" — Paragraph 1
1. **Function:** Restates the headline configuration-search result as the paragraph's starting point, transitioning from Results to interpretation.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The full 1200-configuration evaluation, headline best-performing combination (LAI_model, layer 8, extreme thresholds).
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct restatement, no hedge — a bridging sentence, not yet an explanation.
4. **Literature use:** None.
5. **Certainty level:** N/A — restating an already-established own-result.
6. **Concordant/discordant:** N/A.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Sets up the mechanistic explanation that follows.
8. **Limitations:** None.
9. **Role:** Bridging/orienting within the subsection.

#### §4.2 — Paragraph 2
1. **Function:** Core interpretive/mechanistic — explains WHY the interactive LAI configuration outperforms the fixed one, via a two-way feedback mechanism (LAI affects soil moisture via transpiration; soil moisture affects LAI via water-stress-limited growth), demonstrated with the paper's own time-series data at a specific example site, and validated against an external soil-physics mechanism from prior literature.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The LAI_model vs. LAI_clim structural difference and Fig. 7's time-series comparison at one calibration-subset grid point (Department 31).
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct mechanistic explanation of the bidirectional coupling, unhedged: "The interannual variability in the LAI depends on meteorological conditions and the effect of soil water deficit on photosynthesis and plant growth. At the same time, the simulated soil moisture depends on the amount of water extracted from the soil by plant transpiration... The interactive leaf area index scheme of LAI_model is an improvement over the fixed pattern of LAI_clim because it includes vegetation feedback in response to soil drought." A specific illustrative example from the paper's own data follows: "the lower soil moisture content of LAI_clim in 2003 with respect to LAI_model corresponds to higher LAI values during the warm season. This effect is much more visible for SWI8 than for SWI5." The differential depth-sensitivity of this effect is then explained via an external, established soil-physics mechanism: "As explained by Ravina (1983), the hydraulic conductivity of the topsoil layer decreases with drying to the point where moisture in the deeper layers can remain practically unchanged. Soil moisture variations in deep layers are therefore more dependent on water uptake by roots than on diffusion processes. This explains the large impact of vegetation transpiration and the stronger correlation with the LAI."
4. **Literature use:** Ravina (1983) supplies an established soil-physics mechanism (topsoil hydraulic-conductivity decline with drying) applied here to explain why the LAI-interactivity effect is stronger at depth than near the surface — a clean A3-type (literature-established mechanism applied to a new instance) use, directly grounded in and cross-validated against the paper's own Fig. 7 data rather than substituting for missing own-data (unlike several A3 instances elsewhere in the corpus where the paper's own instrumentation could not measure the mechanism at all — here the paper's own data directly shows the pattern the citation explains).
5. **Certainty level:** Stated with high confidence throughout — appropriate since the mechanism is both a matter of established land-surface-model physics (the model's own equations directly encode the LAI-transpiration-soil-moisture coupling) and directly demonstrated in the paper's own example time series.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** N/A (own-model mechanistic explanation, validated rather than contrasted against Ravina 1983).
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Implicit — establishes the physical basis for the paper's central methodological recommendation (use interactive, not prescribed, LAI).
8. **Limitations:** None in this paragraph.
9. **Role:** Core interpretive/mechanistic, own-data example + literature-validated depth-dependence explanation.

#### §4.2 — Paragraph 3
1. **Function:** Core interpretive — explains WHY deeper soil layers (specifically layer 8) outperform shallower ones for damage prediction, linking a physical noise/signal argument (high-frequency near-surface fluctuations vs. filtered deep-layer trends) directly to the phenomenon's own time-scale (subsidence as slow/gradual), and closes with a general threshold-sensitivity finding.
2. **Finding it starts from:** Fig. 3's finding that all drought-index types correlate better with claims for deep soil layers, with SWI8 the best.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct mechanistic chain, unhedged: "Figure 7 shows that the main difference between the two SWI variables is the presence of a high-frequency component in the SWI5 time series. The proximity of this most superficial layer to the surface... explains these short-term fluctuations, which consequently fade with depth... the SWI of a deep soil layer... reflects longer-term soil moisture trends compared to surface soil moisture. This explains the better correlation of the SWI8-based drought indices with damage data, **considering that** subsidence is a long-term, gradual phenomenon" — the explanation explicitly ties the statistical finding (deep layers correlate better) back to the physical nature of the target phenomenon (subsidence's own slow timescale), a matching-timescales argument rather than a purely statistical one.
4. **Literature use:** None new in this paragraph — self-contained physical reasoning grounded in the paper's own model output (Fig. 7).
5. **Certainty level:** Stated with high confidence, directly grounded in the paper's own comparative data (Fig. 3, Fig. 7) and a physically intuitive timescale-matching argument.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** N/A.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Closes with a general, directly actionable methodological finding: "For all simulations, layers, and index types, better results are observed for low thresholds. Consequently, a daily SWI below the 5th percentile of the empirical distribution is relevant for the definition of drought when monitoring subsidence" — a specific, quantitative, directly actionable recommendation for future index construction.
8. **Limitations:** None in this paragraph.
9. **Role:** Core interpretive/mechanistic, closing §4.2 with a specific, quantitative methodological recommendation.

#### §4.3 "Is the optimal drought magnitude a reliable proxy for subsidence damage?" — Paragraph 1
1. **Function:** Core interpretive — explains WHY magnitude outperforms the two alternative index types (duration, severity) via each alternative's specific statistical weakness, then reports the headline validation statistic with its full significance reporting.
2. **Finding it starts from:** Fig. 3's finding that magnitude outperforms duration and severity as index types, particularly at higher (less extreme) percentile thresholds and shallower layers.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct, index-type-by-index-type mechanistic explanation: "The duration index loses much information for large percentile drought thresholds, hence the lower correlation values. As with severity, this index is inherently sensitive to extreme values, which is not well suited to the frequent soil moisture variations in near-surface layers that are not representative of long-term trends." A convergence caveat is then named directly rather than omitted: "Nevertheless, we note very similar Kendall tau values for the three indices at percentile drought thresholds of less than the 15th percentile" — an honest statement that the magnitude index's advantage is threshold-dependent, not universal. The paragraph then states the design rationale for magnitude's overall selection: "magnitude is chosen as the best index because it combines both duration and severity, compensating for their respective drawbacks."
4. **Literature use:** None new in this paragraph.
5. **Certainty level:** Stated with high confidence, directly grounded in the paper's own comparative statistics (Fig. 3, Fig. 4) — appropriate for a within-study index-type comparison.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** The explicit "nevertheless, very similar... at thresholds less than the 15th percentile" statement is a clean instance of NOT overstating a comparative advantage — the paper names the specific condition under which its preferred index's advantage narrows, rather than presenting a blanket superiority claim.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** The paragraph closes with the headline validation statistic, framed as a direct concordance/robustness claim: "The average Kendall tau of 0.42 between magnitudes and the standardized number of claims for the calibration set shows a strong positive association, with a high statistical significance (p value <= 0.001)."
8. **Limitations:** None new in this paragraph (the threshold-dependence caveat functions more as a scope-precision statement than a limitation).
9. **Role:** Core interpretive, comparative-mechanism explanation (within-study, own alternative index types) + headline validation-statistic reporting.

#### §4.3 — Paragraph 2
1. **Function:** Core interpretive — validates the index's face validity against independently, externally documented drought years, explicitly naming the index's dual capability (detecting both presence and absence of drought).
2. **Finding it starts from:** The index correctly flagging 2003 and 2018 as major drought years at more than half the study sites.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct concordance statement: "These two years experienced notable summer drought events, which **have been widely documented in the literature** (see for example Buras et al., 2020)."
4. **Literature use:** Buras et al. (2020) cited as independent, external confirmation that 2003 and 2018 were indeed major documented drought years — an A1-type concordance-validation use, here validating the index's face validity (does it flag known events?) rather than a numeric-magnitude comparison.
5. **Certainty level:** Stated with high confidence, directly and simply validated against an independent external source.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** Fully concordant.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Implicit — establishes the index's basic face-validity before the following paragraph turns to the harder question of discrepancies.
8. **Limitations:** None new.
9. **Role:** Core interpretive, concordance-validating (A1-type).

#### §4.3 — Paragraph 3
1. **Function:** Discordant-finding handling — names a genuine, two-directional mismatch between the index and the claims data explicitly, and works through a detailed institutional-mechanism explanation for both directions of the mismatch, rather than dismissing either as noise.
2. **Finding it starts from:** Observed discrepancies: drought years with no recorded damage; damage recorded in the absence of drought.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct, unhedged discordance statement: "Nevertheless, we observe discrepancies: drought years with no damage recorded and, conversely, damage recorded in the absence of drought." The explanation is then built entirely around the structure of the French CatNat compensation system (a specific institutional/legal mechanism, not a physical one): a decree must be published for a municipality-year for claims to be recorded at all, and the decree's meteorological criterion is calculated on a SEASONAL basis with a frequency threshold — meaning the two datasets (the paper's own annual magnitude index and the claims record) are gated through structurally different temporal criteria. For the drought-without-damage direction: "A drought event can occur without leading to a decree if the seasonal criteria are not met." For the damage-without-drought direction: "it is possible to have low drought magnitudes and a CatNat decree in the same year. Low threshold magnitudes quantify the driest moments of the year (summer to fall droughts), while a single season only needs to be drier than usual to satisfy the CatNat meteorological criterion. For such years, the presence of claims can be explained by delays in reporting, as it is difficult for both homeowners and insurers to accurately time the occurrence of subsidence damage."
4. **Literature use:** None new in this paragraph — the explanation is built from the paper's own domain knowledge of the CatNat regulatory system, not external citations.
5. **Certainty level:** The institutional-mechanism explanations are stated fairly directly ("This would explain why...") as the most plausible account, appropriately calibrated since these are structural, verifiable facts about a known administrative system (not speculative physical mechanisms), though the specific attribution of any individual discrepant case to reporting delay vs. seasonal-criterion mismatch is not directly testable from the paper's own data and is offered as a plausible account.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** This is the clearest, most systematically developed discordant-finding-handling passage in this paper — both directions of mismatch are named, and each is given its own distinct institutional-mechanism explanation rather than a single blended "measurement noise" dismissal, closing with an explicit acknowledgment that this does not fully explain all discrepancies: "The link to the CatNat regime is not the only factor explaining the inconsistencies between magnitudes and claims: several sources of uncertainty have not been considered in this study. These are discussed in the next section" — a direct, explicit forward-reference bridging to §4.4's systematic uncertainty catalogue.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Implicit — the institutional-mechanism explanation reinforces that the index's imperfect correlation with claims does not necessarily indicate a physical failure of the drought index itself, but rather a structural mismatch with how the validation data are generated — an important scope-clarifying move for how the correlation statistic (tau = 0.42/0.23) should be interpreted.
8. **Limitations bounding:** The explicit forward-reference to §4.4 is a structural bridging device, positioning this paragraph as identifying ONE source of discrepancy (institutional/regulatory) while flagging that others remain to be catalogued — an unusually well-organized limitations architecture relative to the rest of the 15-paper corpus.
9. **Role:** Core interpretive, systematic two-directional discordance handling via institutional-mechanism explanation, explicitly bridging to the dedicated uncertainty-catalogue subsection that follows.

#### §4.4.1 "Representativeness of the insurance database" — Paragraph 1
1. **Function:** Named, locally scoped limitation — states a specific data-quality concern (uneven submission completeness across years/cities) and its size-dependence.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The CCR insurance database's compilation process (aggregated from company submissions).
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct, plainly stated limitation: "the representativeness of these data varies across years and cities, depending on what was submitted. This is truer the smaller the city."
4. **Literature use:** None (self-referential to the Supplement's own data).
5. **Certainty level:** Stated plainly as an acknowledged data-quality property, not hedged as uncertain about whether it exists.
6. **Concordant/discordant:** N/A.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Implicit — one contributing factor (among several catalogued in §4.4) to the imperfect index-claims correlation.
8. **Limitations bounding:** A precise, size-dependence-qualified limitation (worse for smaller cities), not a generic data-quality disclaimer.
9. **Role:** Limitations, first of six systematically organized sub-limitations in §4.4.

#### §4.4.2 "Circularity" — Paragraph 1
