1. **Function:** The paper's most methodologically serious, explicitly self-flagged limitation — names a genuine circularity between the predictor (the paper's own drought index) and the validation target (insurance claims, gated by a decree system that itself depends on a different configuration of the same underlying model), argues for why the circularity is likely weak in practice, then extensively evaluates and ultimately declines an alternative validation data source (InSAR/EGMS) with a detailed, honest justification.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The structural fact that both the paper's magnitude index and the CatNat decree's meteorological criterion are derived from the ISBA model, albeit in different configurations.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct, unhedged admission of the problem: "there is an indirect link between the two variables we are correlating, which induces a certain circularity in the approach. **This weakness is recognized, but no valuable alternative can be considered**: insurance claims are the only available evidence of subsidence, and LSMs are the tools that currently provide the most consistent estimates of soil moisture at the national scale." An argument for why the circularity is likely weak follows, hedged: "**we can consider** the dependency between magnitudes and claims **low**, since the meteorological criterion of the CatNat decree is based on a version of the ISBA model (SWI uniforme) that is very different from the one used in this study and that has been modified several times" — a specific, checkable ground (different model version, repeatedly modified) offered to argue the shared-model-family circularity is not a shared-parameterization circularity. The paragraph then evaluates a genuine alternative — remotely sensed ground motion (InSAR, EGMS) — in detail, citing Burnol et al. (2021) for the general InSAR-subsidence-inference approach and Crosetto et al. (2021) for the EGMS dataset's characteristics, but explicitly declines to use it, citing three specific reasons: signal complexity (non-linear, small-amplitude, spatially heterogeneous, confounded by reflector type per Tzampoglou et al. 2022, and hard to separate from other subsidence causes like water-pumping per Meisina et al. 2006), unavailability at the time of the study, and insufficient temporal overlap (EGMS starts 2018, "which barely overlaps with our study period, which spans 2000 to 2018").
4. **Literature use:** Burnol et al. (2021), Crosetto et al. (2021) introduce and describe the InSAR/EGMS alternative; Tzampoglou et al. (2022) and Meisina et al. (2006) are cited to support why interpreting that alternative would itself be non-trivial (reflector-type confounds; subsidence-cause confounds) — a distinctive citation use, deploying literature to justify NOT adopting an alternative method, i.e., literature supporting a considered-and-declined methodological path, a variant not seen elsewhere in the 15-paper corpus in this specific form (most literature uses either support the chosen method or validate/contrast a finding; here literature is used to responsibly evaluate and reject a rival data source).
5. **Certainty level:** Multi-tiered: the existence of circularity is stated as fact, unhedged; the claim that the circularity is likely weak is explicitly hedged ("we can consider... low") and grounded in a specific, checkable structural difference (different model version); the decision to not use InSAR is presented as a reasoned, evidenced judgment call, not an oversight.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** N/A.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Implicit — the entire passage functions to bound how strongly readers should weight the paper's own headline validation statistic (tau = 0.42), given that predictor and target are not fully independent.
8. **Limitations bounding:** The single most extensively reasoned limitation in this paper's Discussion — a rare instance of a paper not only naming a weakness in its own validation logic but also transparently walking through why a plausible fix (InSAR-based validation) was considered and not adopted, rather than simply noting the fix as unavailable.
9. **Role:** Limitations, most extensively developed single limitation in the corpus for this kind of circularity issue — a distinct sub-type worth noting for Step 5/6: a limitation that considers and explicitly declines a specific alternative methodology, with reasons.

#### §4.4.3 "Differences in temporal and spatial resolution" — Paragraphs 1-3
1. **Function:** Named limitation (temporal-resolution mismatch between annual claims data and daily soil-moisture simulation), immediately paired with an explicit compensating benefit of the finer-resolution simulation data, plus a second, separately treated limitation (spatial-resolution mismatch, judged to be practically minor).
2. **Finding it starts from:** The annual granularity of insurance-claim reporting versus the daily granularity of the ISBA simulation; the municipal-scale claims data versus the 8 km ISBA grid.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct limitation-with-cause statement: "The annual temporal resolution of this study is forced by the insurance claim data set... The coarse resolution of the first data set is explained by the difficulty for insurers of precisely dating the occurrence of subsidence damage due to the slow nature of the phenomenon." A compensating benefit of the finer daily data is then named explicitly: "there is an advantage to using daily instead of annual soil moisture data: the SWI distribution is expected to be more robust over a 19-year period due to the large number of observations" — a limitation-then-partial-compensation move (C2-type). A residual, unresolved uncertainty is still named directly: "The hypothesis of a stable daily soil moisture distribution remains a source of uncertainty inherent to this work." For spatial resolution: "the number of claims is available at the municipal scale, while soil moisture is modeled at an 8 km x 8 km scale. These two grids are of the same order of magnitude" — explicitly judged as a minor, practically acceptable mismatch rather than a significant limitation.
4. **Literature use:** None in this passage.
5. **Certainty level:** The temporal-resolution limitation and its explanation are stated plainly; the residual stable-distribution-hypothesis uncertainty is explicitly and directly flagged as unresolved ("remains a source of uncertainty"), not glossed over even after the compensating-benefit argument.
6. **Concordant/discordant:** N/A.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** None explicit — purely limitation-bounding content.
8. **Limitations bounding:** Two distinct limitations (temporal, spatial), one with an explicit partial compensation plus residual uncertainty (temporal), one judged and explicitly stated to be practically minor (spatial) — a clear instance of NOT treating every named limitation as equally weighty; the paper explicitly distinguishes a limitation it considers consequential from one it does not.
9. **Role:** Limitations, third and fourth of six systematic sub-limitations.

#### §4.4.4 "Cumulative effect of subsidence" — Paragraph 1
1. **Function:** Named limitation (single-year-only index construction, ignoring multi-year cumulative drought effects), paired with an explicit, evidence-based judgment that the omission is not severely damaging the index's performance, plus a specific future-improvement suggestion.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The physical possibility that drought effects on clay soils accumulate across consecutive dry years, not resetting each year.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** A mechanistic justification for why cumulative effects matter physically is given first, citing prior literature: "the soil under perennial plants is likely to be so dry that a single wet season may not be able to compensate for the water deficit. This leads to a cumulative effect of droughts and thus soil movement (Page, 1998)." The paper then explicitly names its own simplification and evaluates its practical consequence using its own already-reported validation result: "The cumulative effect is therefore neglected and is a source of uncertainty. **The good agreement obtained here between drought magnitudes and normalized claims indicates that** the conditions of a single year are a satisfactory enough predictor of subsidence occurrence" — using the paper's own already-established headline result (tau = 0.42) as evidence that the omitted mechanism, while real, is not severely undermining the index's practical performance, a distinctive self-referential validation move.
4. **Literature use:** Page (1998) supports the general physical existence of cumulative drought/subsidence effects — an A3-type attributed-mechanism use, here explicitly named as an OMITTED factor rather than one the paper incorporates.
5. **Certainty level:** The cumulative-effect mechanism itself is treated as established (per Page 1998, unhedged); the judgment that its omission is "satisfactory enough" is a moderate-confidence inference drawn from the paper's own aggregate correlation statistic, not a direct test of the cumulative-effect hypothesis itself.
6. **Concordant/discordant:** N/A.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** A specific, concrete future-improvement suggestion closes the paragraph: "This step could be implemented in a damage model by, for example, weighting magnitudes by their history" — a specific, technically actionable methodological recommendation.
8. **Limitations bounding:** A limitation named, then evaluated (not merely acknowledged) using the paper's own already-reported result as evidence the omission is tolerable — a distinctive, evidence-based (not merely asserted) limitation-severity judgment.
9. **Role:** Limitations, fifth of six sub-limitations, paired with a self-referential severity assessment and a specific future-work suggestion.

#### §4.4.5 "Zoning of clay shrink-swell exposure" — Paragraph 1
1. **Function:** Named limitation (coarse-resolution exposure/hazard zoning map used for the normalization step), explicitly assessed for its severity relative to the paper's own stated scope, plus a distinct, separately named omission (deformation intensity not modeled).
2. **Finding it starts from:** The 1:50,000-scale clay-exposure map used to select/normalize the housing stock analyzed.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct limitation statement: "The blending of moderate- and high-exposure zones... is an initial source of uncertainty, as the same drought is expected to trigger different responses in soils of different susceptibility... The exposure map... does not integrate very local clayey soil occurrences" (citing BRGM 2024 for the geological basis of this local heterogeneity). The paper then explicitly, directly scopes the severity of this limitation relative to its own research question: "At this stage, we are not trying to make precise damage predictions but rather only identify drought years. Therefore, this source of uncertainty is not the most dominant" — an explicit, self-assessed relative-severity ranking among the paper's own catalogued uncertainties, a notably systematic practice not commonly seen elsewhere in the corpus (most papers name limitations without explicitly ranking their relative severity against each other). A second, distinct omission is named: "this study does not take into account the intensity of the deformation induced by the shrink-swell mechanism, which is a significant component explaining the deformations of the structures."
4. **Literature use:** BRGM (2024) supports the geological basis for local clay-occurrence heterogeneity not captured by the regional map.
5. **Certainty level:** Both limitations stated plainly; the relative-severity judgment ("not the most dominant") is an explicit, reasoned claim tied directly to the paper's own stated scope (drought-year identification, not damage-magnitude prediction).
6. **Concordant/discordant:** N/A.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** None explicit — pure limitation-bounding and scope-clarification.
8. **Limitations bounding:** Notable for explicitly and comparatively ranking this limitation's severity against the paper's OTHER catalogued limitations (via its stated research scope), rather than presenting each limitation as equally weighty — a distinguishing, more sophisticated limitations-architecture practice.
9. **Role:** Limitations, sixth of six, explicit relative-severity self-assessment.

#### §4.4.6 "Local factors" — Paragraph 1
1. **Function:** Named, itemized limitation (three distinct categories of un-modeled local/building-level factors), each supported by its own literature citation, closing with an explicit statement of what future work could and could not address each factor.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The paper's own municipal-scale (not building-scale) analysis, which cannot capture building- or site-specific vulnerability factors.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** A structured, bulleted list (unusual formatting for this corpus — a direct itemization rather than prose-chained factors) of three local-factor categories: vegetation/root effects (citing Freeman et al. 1992; Hawkins 2013; Page 1998), building characteristics (citing Page 1998), and site/environmental characteristics (citing Cooling and Ward 1948; Page 1998). The paragraph closes with a direct, differentiated statement of feasibility for future work: "The specific effect of trees on soil moisture fluctuations could be investigated by modifying the ISBA model configuration. For all the other local factors, evaluation of their influence on subsidence is only possible through local analysis using instrumented sites" — explicitly distinguishing a factor that COULD be addressed within the paper's own existing modeling framework (vegetation/roots, via ISBA reconfiguration) from factors that would require an entirely different research design (site instrumentation) to address.
4. **Literature use:** Freeman et al. (1992), Hawkins (2013), Page (1998), Cooling and Ward (1948) — each supporting one specific, named local factor as a documented contributor to subsidence vulnerability, none of them tested or measured by this paper's own data — a clean, explicitly bounded set of A3-type attributed (not this-paper's-own) mechanisms.
5. **Certainty level:** Each factor's general existence/relevance is stated as established (per the cited literature), while the paper's own inability to test any of them is stated plainly and without hedging as a direct consequence of its own data's spatial granularity (municipal, not building-level).
6. **Concordant/discordant:** N/A.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** The differentiated feasibility statement (some factors addressable via model reconfiguration; others requiring instrumented field sites) functions as a scoped, practically differentiated research-agenda implication, rather than a single undifferentiated "more research is needed" statement.
8. **Limitations bounding:** The most itemized, structurally explicit limitation passage in the paper (and among the most explicit in the 15-paper corpus), closing §4.4's uncertainty catalogue.
9. **Role:** Limitations, closing the six-part uncertainty catalogue with a differentiated, feasibility-scoped research-agenda statement.

#### §4.5 "What are the possible applications... in and outside of France?" — Paragraph 1
1. **Function:** Transitional/orienting for the closing subsection — restates the paper's central contribution and explicitly, briefly reasserts that it "faces several limitations," bridging from the uncertainty catalogue just completed to the forward-looking applications discussion.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The paper's own central result (the yearly drought magnitude index, calibrated/validated against insurance data).
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct restatement, unhedged for the core contribution ("this index is relevant for the identification of subsidence-induced drought events"), immediately and briefly qualified: "It faces several limitations due to the various sources of uncertainty" — a compact backward-reference to §4.4 rather than a re-litigation of each limitation individually.
4. **Literature use:** None in this specific paragraph.
5. **Certainty level:** The core contribution is stated with confidence; the limitations reference is brief and general, appropriately deferring the detail to the already-completed §4.4.
6. **Concordant/discordant:** N/A.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Sets up the applications discussion.
8. **Limitations:** A compact backward-reference, not a new limitation.
9. **Role:** Bridging/orienting for the closing subsection.

#### §4.5 — Paragraph 2
1. **Function:** Core practical-implication paragraph — proposes two distinct, concrete applications (national drought monitoring; an improved predictor within an existing named insurance-industry loss model), each addressed to a specific, named category of user.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The paper's own demonstrated index performance, generalized from the 20-city sample to the whole country.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct, unhedged generalization: "The research was carried out on a sample of 20 cities, but the index can be calculated for the whole country" — a scope-extension claim grounded in the fact that the underlying ISBA simulation already runs nationally (an 8 km grid), not merely asserted without basis. Two applications follow, each named specifically: monitoring ("as is currently done by French institutions" — implying continuity with existing practice, not a novel deployment context) and insurance-industry loss modeling, with a specific named existing model as the integration target: "as an improved predictor in subsidence loss models, such as the one developed by Charpentier et al. (2022)."
4. **Literature use:** Charpentier et al. (2022) (previously cited in §4.1 for the SPI/SSWI divergence finding) is re-cited here for a distinct purpose — naming a specific, existing operational model into which the paper's own index could be integrated, a genuine B2-type dual-use-of-one-citation instance (methodological-comparison use in §4.1; practical-integration-target use in §4.5).
5. **Certainty level:** The national-scalability claim is stated with confidence, grounded in the ISBA simulation's existing national coverage (a structural fact, not a speculative extrapolation); the two application proposals are stated as concrete possibilities, appropriately registered as forward-looking recommendations rather than demonstrated deployments.
6. **Concordant/discordant:** N/A.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** This paragraph IS the paper's direct, addressed-to-named-stakeholder-categories practical implication (French monitoring institutions; the insurance industry) — a genuinely actionable, decision-relevant implication (informing which years/areas warrant monitoring attention or claim scrutiny), consistent with the A5 pattern (implications track whether the finding bears on an actionable quantity a stakeholder can use) established in the original 9-paper file.
8. **Limitations:** None new.
9. **Role:** Core practical implication, addressed to two distinct, named stakeholder categories.

#### §4.5 — Paragraph 3 (closing)
1. **Function:** Closing paragraph for the Discussion — generalizes the paper's methodology beyond France to other countries facing the same hazard, citing two specific named international comparator studies, and closes on the specific technical condition required for that generalization (availability of high-resolution atmospheric forcing).
2. **Finding it starts from:** The paper's own French-specific demonstration, extended to an international context.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct generalization claim, supported by naming specific other affected countries and their own documented clay-shrinkage-subsidence problems: "As several regions worldwide face similar clay-shrinkage-induced subsidence problems (see for example MacQueen et al., 2023, and Mostafiz et al., 2021, for the UK and the USA, respectively), the methodology can be adapted to other countries." The paper closes with an explicit, precise technical precondition for this generalization: "The SURFEX modeling platform, in which ISBA is implemented, can be applied anywhere, provided that high-resolution atmospheric forcing is available" — a scoped, conditional generalization claim, not an unconditional one.
4. **Literature use:** MacQueen et al. (2023), Mostafiz et al. (2021) support the claim that clay-shrinkage subsidence is a documented problem in other specific countries (UK, USA respectively) — a scope-extending, motivating citation use for the generalization claim, not a concordance/discordance validation of this paper's own numeric findings.
5. **Certainty level:** The generalization claim is explicitly and precisely conditioned ("provided that high-resolution atmospheric forcing is available") rather than stated as an unconditional capability — an appropriately scoped closing claim.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** N/A.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** This closing paragraph is itself a scoped, conditional generalizability implication — the Discussion ends on an affirmative, forward-looking statement of the method's broader applicability, explicitly bounded by a stated technical precondition rather than an unconditional promotional claim.
8. **Limitations:** None new — though the explicit precondition functions as an implicit scope-boundary on the generalization claim.
9. **Role:** Closing — an affirmative-synthesis-and-broadening close (closing-move type (a) in the original 9-paper file's Step 4 typology: reassertion of the paper's contribution, generalized outward), explicitly and precisely conditioned rather than an unqualified promotional statement, ending the Discussion before Section 5's Conclusion opens with "In this paper, we propose a new annual drought index, the optimal drought magnitude, tailored to the subsidence hazard."

**Note on this paper's structural character:** This Discussion is organized as a sequence of explicitly posed, named questions (subsection titles function as literal questions: "Why is an interactive LAI needed?", "Is the optimal drought magnitude a reliable proxy for subsidence damage?", "What are the sources of uncertainty?", "What are the possible applications...?") — a distinctive, unusually transparent organizing structure not seen with this explicitness elsewhere in the 15-paper corpus, though functionally similar in spirit to the per-city or per-mechanism organizing logic of other papers. §4.4 ("Sources of uncertainty") is the single most systematically organized, itemized limitations catalogue in the entire 15-paper corpus — six named, distinct sub-limitations, each independently developed, with at least two instances of the paper explicitly and comparatively ranking a limitation's severity against its own stated research scope or against its other limitations (a genuinely new practice not clearly documented in the original 9-paper file or the other five new papers) and one instance (§4.4.2, circularity) of a limitation passage that explicitly evaluates and declines a specific alternative methodology (InSAR/EGMS validation) with detailed reasoning rather than simply noting an alternative's unavailability. This paper is also notable for a limitation-then-workaround move (C2-type) at §4.4.3 (temporal-resolution mismatch, compensated by daily data's improved distributional robustness) paired transparently with a residual, explicitly unresolved uncertainty in the same passage — a nuanced middle case between "limitation fully compensated" and "limitation left fully open."
## PAPER 15: Martins et al. 2024 — Satellite view of the 2022 European heatwave

**Citation:** Martins, J. P. A., Caetano, S., Pereira, C., Dutra, E., and Cardoso, R. M. (2024). *A satellite view of the exceptionally warm summer of 2022 over Europe.* Nat. Hazards Earth Syst. Sci., 24, 1501– (2024). URL: https://nhess.copernicus.org/articles/24/1501/2024/

**Status: SUCCESS, with an important structural caveat flagged explicitly.** Full research article confirmed. This paper has NO section named "Discussion" anywhere, and — unlike every other paper in the 15-paper set, including the other merged-section papers (Shahi, Li, Cavalié) — it does not even have a section titled "Results and discussion." Its four top-level sections are: `1 Introduction`, `2 Data and methods`, `3 Results`, `4 Conclusions`. Verbatim recovery: complete text of Results §3.5 "Exceptionality of the 2022 heatwave" (confirmed verbatim, one WebFetch call) and complete text of the entire `4 Conclusions` section (confirmed verbatim, one WebFetch call, five paragraphs). Per the task's explicit instruction to identify which structural type applies (standalone vs. merged) rather than force this paper into either the "standalone Discussion" or "Results-and-discussion merged" categories used for the other 14 papers, this paper is scored as **a third, distinct structural type: Discussion content distributed between the tail of the Results section (§3.5, containing genuine interpretive content per the task's own initial verification pass, alongside descriptive reporting) and a merged Conclusions section that performs Discussion-type interpretive work (mechanism explanation, cross-dataset concordance/discordance handling, forward-looking framing) without ever being named "Discussion."** This is treated as a genuinely new position-testability case in Step 3/4, not folded into the existing "merged Results-and-discussion" count without comment.

### STEP 1 — Foundations
- **Research question:** Was the summer 2022 heatwave over Europe truly exceptional relative to the full 2004-2023 satellite land-surface-temperature (LST) record, across multiple independent heatwave metrics? Does satellite-derived LST provide meaningfully different or complementary information to standard 2 m-temperature-based reanalysis diagnostics (ERA5/ERA5-Land), particularly under drought/vegetation-stress conditions?
- **Objective:** Use the LSA SAF all-sky LST satellite product (2004 onward), combined with H SAF soil-wetness-index and LSA SAF vegetation-cover products, to characterize the 2022 European summer heatwave's magnitude, spatial extent, and duration across four independent metrics (mean LST anomaly, area under extreme heat, number of hot days, Heatwave Magnitude Index/HWMI), benchmark 2022 against every other year 2004-2023, and cross-compare LST-based diagnostics against ERA5/ERA5-Land skin- and 2 m-temperature-based diagnostics to assess where and why the two data sources agree or diverge.
- **Study design:** Empirical satellite-remote-sensing and reanalysis comparison study (not an experiment) — LSA SAF MLST-ASv2 all-sky LST (2004-2023), H SAF soil wetness index, LSA SAF fraction-of-vegetation-cover, compared against ERA5 (synoptic/atmospheric context) and ERA5-Land (2 m temperature, skin temperature); heatwave metrics defined via a 90th-percentile hot-day threshold (relative to the 2004-2021 reference period) and a magnitude index (HWMI) summing standardized daily temperature anomalies over qualifying heatwave periods.
- **Population/sample:** Not a human population — the full Meteosat Second Generation (MSG) satellite coverage area over Europe; temporal sample is summer (JJA) 2004-2023, with 2022 as the year of primary interest.
- **Key findings the Discussion-equivalent content must interpret:**
  - 2022 ranked first among all 20 years in all four independent heatwave metrics: mean LST anomaly (2.2°C, vs. 1.1°C for the next-ranked years, 2018/2019), mean area under extreme heat (just above 2.5% of Europe), mean number of hot days (~21, vs. ~14 for second-ranked 2012), and area-averaged HWMI (22, vs. 11.9/11.7 for 2012/2018).
  - The four rankings show "considerable disparities" among each other for most years (i.e., a year that ranks high on one metric does not necessarily rank high on another), even though 2022 and 2018 consistently top all four.
  - LST anomalies were amplified specifically in areas under severe drought/vegetation stress (low soil wetness index, low fraction-of-vegetation-cover), a pattern not captured by ERA5-Land's 2 m/skin temperature, attributed to ERA5-Land's lack of dynamic vegetation representation.
  - The satellite LST record's relatively short length (starting 2004) is explicitly noted as excluding the well-documented 2003 European heatwave from the multi-year ranking comparison — a named dataset limitation.

### STEP 2 — Discussion-equivalent paragraph-by-paragraph (Results §3.5 interpretive content, then the full Conclusions section)

#### Results §3.5, final paragraph (dataset-length caveat)
1. **Function:** Although located within the Results section (not Discussion/Conclusions), this specific closing passage of §3.5 performs a genuine limitation-bounding, Discussion-type function — it is flagged and analyzed here explicitly because the task requires locating "the actual Discussion section... may be merged," and this is the paper's clearest instance of a self-aware, locally-placed limitation statement outside the Conclusions.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The temperature-record-mapping exercise (Fig. 11) showing which year set the all-time-record JJA anomaly in each grid cell, 2004-2023.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct factual note, followed by an explicit limitation statement: "It is worth noting that 2003 set JJA temperature records over large areas of western Europe, but since that year is not within the period covered by the LSA SAF LST dataset, it does not show up in Fig. 11. **This can be regarded as a caveat to this dataset, since due to its relatively short length, it does not allow for a full picture of the most relevant extreme heat events over Europe** for the past couple of decades."
4. **Literature use:** None in this specific passage (the 2003 heatwave's severity is treated as common/established knowledge, not attributed to a specific citation here).
5. **Certainty level:** Stated plainly and directly as an acknowledged dataset-length limitation, not hedged as uncertain about whether the limitation exists.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** N/A.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Implicit — bounds how the paper's own "2022 ranks first in all metrics, 2004-2023" claim should be read: as a claim about the satellite-LST-record era specifically, not an all-time claim (2003 might rival or exceed 2022 by some metrics, but cannot be directly compared using this paper's own primary dataset).
8. **Limitations bounding:** A precise, locally placed limitation (record starts 2004, excluding a known major prior event) — structurally identical in placement-logic to the A4a pattern (local limitation placement) from the original 9-paper file, even though it occurs in the Results section rather than a Discussion section, because this paper's Discussion-type content is not concentrated in one place.
9. **Role:** Locally placed limitation, Results-embedded (an important structural note for Step 3/4: this paper's interpretive/limitation content is NOT confined to its Conclusions section).

#### §4 Conclusions — Paragraph 1
1. **Function:** Opening/orienting for the Discussion-equivalent content — restates the headline exceptionality finding, then moves directly into a mechanistic (synoptic-meteorological) explanation and an explicit statement of the current theoretical understanding's completeness/incompleteness.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The cumulative Results findings on 2022's exceptionality.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct restatement, unhedged ("The year 2022 was exceptional for Europe..."), followed by a mechanistic attribution to synoptic drivers: "The dominant synoptic configuration caused unprecedented blocking, subsidence heating and warm advection into the continent, as well as a prolonged record drought from spring onwards." An explicit statement of theoretical completeness follows, carefully hedged: "**Although some uncertainties persist**, a theoretical framework is **already able to broadly explain** the broad mechanisms involved in the role of the surface–atmosphere coupling in the modulation of these events."
4. **Literature use:** None in this specific paragraph (the synoptic/blocking mechanism and the surface-atmosphere-coupling framework are referenced as established background knowledge, without a specific citation attached in the verbatim text recovered).
5. **Certainty level:** Explicitly and carefully calibrated: the theoretical framework is stated as "broadly" explanatory, not fully explanatory, with an upfront hedge ("although some uncertainties persist") — an honest, appropriately bounded certainty statement rather than an overclaim of complete mechanistic understanding.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** N/A.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Closing sentence generalizes beyond the single 2022 case: "The combination of these ingredients has been key in setting the magnitude of the 2022 heatwave as well as that of other recent mega heatwaves" — a direct generalization to a broader event class, appropriately scoped ("other recent mega heatwaves," not "all heatwaves").
8. **Limitations bounding:** The "although some uncertainties persist" hedge functions as a compact, locally placed limitation on the theoretical-mechanism claim, immediately adjacent to the claim it qualifies.
9. **Role:** Opening/orienting for the Discussion-equivalent content, mechanistic + explicitly bounded certainty statement.

#### §4 Conclusions — Paragraph 2
1. **Function:** Core interpretive — restates the paper's headline quantitative findings across all four metrics in one consolidated passage, then addresses a potential discordant/complicating sub-finding (some areas' single-month records were set in 2023, not 2022) and explicitly resolves it in favor of the paper's central claim.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The four-metric ranking results (LST anomaly, area under extreme heat, hot-day count, HWMI) and the JJA-vs-monthly record-year discrepancy noted in Fig. 11's discussion.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct, unhedged restatement of all four headline statistics in sequence, then an explicit acknowledgment of a potentially complicating finding, immediately resolved: "**despite** some areas in June and August having their highest monthly anomalies in 2023, summer 2022 as a whole **remains** exceptionally warm, ranking first in all considered metrics" — a concession-then-reaffirmation structure (a compact version of the "steelman-then-rebut" discordant-handling move, C1-adjacent, seen more elaborately in Nguyen [Ca Mau]'s sediment-compensation paragraph in the original 9-paper file).
4. **Literature use:** None in this paragraph.
5. **Certainty level:** Stated with high confidence throughout — appropriate since these are directly measured, ranked statistics from the paper's own primary dataset.
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** A genuine, specific discordant sub-finding (2023 beating 2022 in some areas for some individual months) is named directly rather than omitted, and is explicitly distinguished from the paper's own primary claim (JJA-season-aggregate exceptionality, not every sub-period/sub-region without exception) — a precise scope clarification rather than a denial of the discordant data point.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** Implicit — reinforces the robustness of the "2022 was exceptional" claim by showing it survives a specific potential counter-example.
8. **Limitations:** None new.
9. **Role:** Core interpretive, consolidating restatement + concession-then-reaffirmation discordance handling.

#### §4 Conclusions — Paragraph 3
1. **Function:** Core interpretive/mechanistic — the paper's central methodological-contribution argument, explaining WHY LST provides complementary (not merely redundant) information relative to standard 2 m-temperature products, via a direct comparison of each data source's error-generating process, then linking a specific new finding (LST-anomaly amplification under drought/vegetation stress) to a proposed use (as a proxy for surface-atmosphere coupling), closing with an explicit, precise technical caveat about the LST retrieval's own internal dependency on soil-moisture input.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The general architecture of T2m-based products (station-interpolated or model-assimilated) versus satellite LST (spatially continuous, minimal interpolation error) and the specific finding that LST-anomaly amplification co-occurs with low SWI/low FVC.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** A direct comparative-mechanism argument: T2m products' errors are attributed to a named process ("These methods introduce interpolation and model errors into the spatially continuous T2m fields"); satellite LST's advantage is attributed to a different named process ("spatial coverage is much higher, and therefore spatial interpolation errors are mostly absent"), immediately and honestly qualified: "although there are relatively small uncertainties associated with geolocation and regridding." The paper then states its own new empirical finding and its proposed interpretation, moderately hedged: "when there are strong drought conditions linked with vegetation anomalies..., differences between LSTMax and T2m anomalies are amplified, and therefore these results **highlight that** these observed differences **may be used as** proxies for surface–atmosphere coupling metrics." The paragraph closes with a precise, technically specific limitation on this very same LST product: "It should be noted however that SWI is used as input to the cloudy-sky LST retrieval. However, most of the retrievals under heatwave conditions are made under clear skies, so most of the LST signal comes from the infrared retrieval and not from the surface energy balance scheme used for cloudy-sky retrievals" — a precise circularity-avoidance clarification (the LST-SWI relationship being interpreted as physically meaningful is NOT an artifact of SWI being an input to LST's own retrieval algorithm, because the clear-sky retrieval pathway used during heatwave conditions does not depend on SWI) — closely analogous in structure and rigor to the explicit circularity-check performed in Barthelemy et al. (§4.4.2, Paper 14, this batch), though here the circularity is checked and found NOT to apply (a genuinely different, reassuring outcome) rather than acknowledged as a real, if weak, dependency.
4. **Literature use:** None new in this specific paragraph (E-OBS and ERA5 are named as examples of T2m-product types, not cited as comparator studies to validate/contrast against).
5. **Certainty level:** Multi-tiered: the general T2m-vs-LST error-source comparison is stated with high confidence (established data-processing facts); the drought-amplification/surface-coupling-proxy interpretation is explicitly hedged ("highlight that... may be used as"); the closing circularity-check is stated with high confidence, since it is a direct, checkable fact about the retrieval algorithm's own logic (which pathway — clear-sky vs. cloudy-sky — actually applies under the conditions being studied).
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** N/A within this paragraph (a mechanistic/comparative-advantage argument, not a concordance/discordance-of-findings comparison).
7. **Interpretation→implication:** The drought/vegetation-stress proxy proposal is the paper's central scientific-contribution implication — a moderately hedged, appropriately scoped claim about a new potential diagnostic use for the LST-T2m difference signal, not yet a fully validated operational tool.
8. **Limitations bounding:** Two distinct, locally placed technical limitations in one paragraph: the geolocation/regridding uncertainty (brief, parenthetical) and the more substantial SWI-circularity clarification (extended, precise) — both placed immediately adjacent to the specific claims they qualify.
9. **Role:** Core interpretive/mechanistic, comparative-advantage argument + a rare, explicit and successfully-resolved (not merely flagged) circularity check.

#### §4 Conclusions — Paragraph 4
1. **Function:** Core interpretive — a direct concordance-and-discordance analysis comparing the paper's LSTMax-based metrics against ERA5-Land's SKTMax-based metrics, explicitly naming where they diverge and providing a specific, checkable mechanistic reason for the divergence (a named model deficiency — lack of dynamic vegetation representation), then broadening to a general cross-dataset consistency argument.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The comparison between LSTMax-based and SKTMax(ERA5-Land)-based heatwave diagnostics, including specific negative anomalies over central Europe and the Hungary-Romania region.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** A concordance-first framing, but immediately and explicitly distinguished from a "reject the comparator on any mismatch" logic: "the comparisons of LSTMax- and SKTMax-based metrics further **reinforced the confidence** of the former, **not only because they compare relatively well in general but also because when they do not, there are plausible reasons for it**" — a distinctive, explicit statement of the paper's own epistemic standard for validation: agreement is evidence of validity, but so is a well-explained, mechanistically grounded disagreement, rather than disagreement being treated as undermining confidence in either dataset. The specific mechanism for the ERA5-Land divergence is then named directly: "ERA5-Land does not rely on dynamic vegetation information, and therefore strong negative anomalies... are not well represented by the model. This means that surface roughness and evapotranspiration efficiency are too high in the model, leading to colder SKTs when compared to the observed LSTs." The paragraph then broadens to a second, independent line of cross-validating evidence: "the cross-cutting analysis comparing LST, FVC and SWI anomalies shows a remarkable physical consistency between the observed patterns, thus reinforcing confidence in these datasets."
4. **Literature use:** None new in this specific paragraph — the ERA5-Land vegetation-representation deficiency is stated as the paper's own diagnosed explanation, not attributed to an external source in the recovered verbatim text.
5. **Certainty level:** The general concordance claim and the specific ERA5-Land mechanism explanation are both stated with high, direct confidence — an appropriately confident register given the mechanism (ERA5-Land's static-vegetation limitation) is a well-known, documented property of that specific reanalysis product, and the paper's own data directly shows the predicted consequence (colder SKT in exactly the areas with strong drought/vegetation-stress signal).
6. **Concordant/discordant handling:** This is the clearest, most explicitly reasoned discordant-finding-handling passage in this paper — the paper does not merely note a discrepancy and move on, nor does it treat the discrepancy as damaging to its own LST product's credibility; instead it explicitly reframes a well-explained discrepancy as ADDITIONAL supporting evidence for the LST product's reliability (since the reanalysis's failure mode is independently predictable from a known structural limitation of that specific product) — a genuinely distinctive epistemic move, worth flagging as a candidate refinement of the A2 pattern for Step 5/6 (a discordance is not just tolerated/explained but actively used to argue FOR, not merely alongside, the paper's own preferred data source's validity).
7. **Interpretation→implication:** The closing sentence functions as a direct implication for the broader climate-data-user community: "This kind of analysis is key to fostering their usage for all sorts of climate applications, thus increasing user uptake of these datasets" — a research-infrastructure/data-adoption implication, a distinctive register not seen elsewhere in the 15-paper corpus (most implications are physical/hazard-management-oriented; here the implication is about encouraging broader use of a scientific data product).
8. **Limitations:** None new in this paragraph (the ERA5-Land deficiency is framed as an explanation for the comparator's divergence, not as a limitation of this paper's own primary LST dataset).
9. **Role:** Core interpretive, discordance-as-supporting-evidence (a distinctive variant of A2) + cross-dataset triangulation + data-adoption implication.

#### §4 Conclusions — Paragraph 5 (closing)
1. **Function:** Closing paragraph — states a specific, named limitation of the underlying satellite data record (not yet long enough for formal 30-year climate normals), immediately reframed as not undermining the record's current value, and closes with a forward-looking statement of the dataset's broader utility for future climate monitoring.
2. **Finding it starts from:** The MSG-based LST data record's length (2004-2023, less than 20 years at the time of writing) relative to the WMO-standard 30-year climate-normal convention.
3. **Mechanism of the move:** Direct limitation-then-reassurance structure: "Although the MSG-based LST data record is not still at the stage of being used to derive fully compliant climate normals (which typically use 30 years of data), it **already provides useful perspectives to complement** the study and monitoring of decadal surface temperature variability" — the limitation is named plainly and not minimized (the record genuinely cannot yet support formal climate normals), but immediately bounded by a precise statement of what it CAN still support (decadal-variability studies, surface-atmosphere-coupling diagnostics, diagnosing caveats in other standard datasets) — a clean instance of the C3-type two-tier can-show/cannot-show bounding pattern from the original 9-paper file.
4. **Literature use:** None in this paragraph.
5. **Certainty level:** The record-length limitation is stated as an objective fact (a straightforward comparison against the WMO 30-year convention), not hedged as uncertain; the "already provides useful perspectives" claim is stated with moderate-high confidence, directly grounded in the specific applications just demonstrated in this very paper (surface-atmosphere-coupling diagnosis, cross-dataset caveat-detection).
6. **Concordant/discordant:** N/A.
7. **Interpretation→implication:** The paragraph's final clause functions as the paper's own forward-looking research/utility statement: "These include the study of surface–atmosphere coupling within extreme heat events based on observations and diagnosing caveats in more standard datasets used for the monitoring [of] those events" — directly echoing and generalizing the specific findings just established (the LST-vs-SKT divergence diagnosis in Paragraph 4) into a stated general capability of the dataset going forward.
8. **Limitations bounding:** A precisely scoped, two-tier limitation (cannot yet support formal 30-year climate normals; can already support decadal-variability and cross-dataset-diagnostic applications) — the paper's clearest, most textbook instance of the C3 two-tier bounding pattern, closing the Discussion-equivalent content on this explicitly bounded but affirmative note rather than an unresolved caveat.
9. **Role:** Closing — an affirmative-synthesis-with-explicit-bounding close (a hybrid of closing-move type (a), reassertion of the paper's/dataset's contribution, and the C3 two-tier limitation-bounding discipline), ending the paper's Discussion-equivalent content on a forward-looking, application-oriented note rather than either an unresolved limitation or a pure forward-research call.

**Note on this paper's structural character:** This is the single most structurally distinctive paper in the full 15-paper corpus regarding WHERE its Discussion-type content lives. It has no section named "Discussion" and no section named "Results and discussion" — its interpretive content is split between a brief but genuine limitation-bounding passage embedded in the tail of the Results section (§3.5, analyzed above) and a Conclusions section that performs the full range of Discussion-type rhetorical work (mechanistic explanation, concordance/discordance handling, an explicit and successfully-resolved circularity check, two-tier limitation bounding) without ever using Discussion-typical section language. This matters directly for the Step 3 position-testability base: this paper cannot be cleanly counted as either "standalone Discussion" or "Results-and-discussion merged" in the same sense as the other 14 papers, and is flagged as its own category for any position-dependent pattern test (A4a/A4b closing-move analysis). Its single most distinctive rhetorical contribution to the corpus is Paragraph 4's discordance-as-supporting-evidence move — treating a well-explained disagreement between two data sources as evidence FOR (not merely compatible with) the paper's own preferred data source's reliability, a genuinely new refinement of the A2 discordant-finding-handling pattern worth testing explicitly in Step 3.
---

## STEP 3 — Cross-Paper Comparison (15 papers)

### Threshold convention (stated before use)

The 9-paper file fixed "recurring" at two-thirds (chosen over 75% as too strict, and over a bare majority as too weak to call a field convention). This update **keeps two-thirds for n=15, which lands at ≥10/15**, specifically to preserve comparability with the 9-paper analysis rather than as a fresh choice — switching thresholds mid-series would make "strengthened/weakened" claims about individual patterns impossible to state cleanly. As before, every pattern reports the **raw count out of 15** first; where a design exclusion is claimed, the **adjusted count over the design-eligible denominator** is also reported explicitly.

**One additional discipline, new at n=15 and required by how varied the 6 new papers turned out to be structurally:** A1 (concordance-first validation) is defined, unchanged from the 9-paper file, as a check against an **independent, external, prior measurement or result** — a citation to a different, previously published study. Two of the six new papers (Strozzi et al., Martins et al.) perform an extensive, evidentially strong validation move that checks a paper's own headline finding against an independent measurement, but the comparator is a *different instrument or dataset within the same paper* (satellite InSAR vs. terrestrial GNSS/GB-DInSAR/MTS for Strozzi; satellite LST vs. ERA5-Land reanalysis for Martins), not a different published study. This is **not counted toward A1** — widening A1's definition mid-series to include internal cross-instrument validation would retroactively make the original file's exclusion of Yuen (no own-finding to validate) and its treatment of Ca Mau (a genuine non-conformer, scored N because no independent prior measurement of CMP inundation exists to check against) internally inconsistent, since both papers arguably could not perform internal cross-validation either, for different design reasons. Instead, this internal-cross-instrument-validation move is tracked as **a new, distinct pattern (D1)**, reported on its own count, below.

**Position-testability base, substantially reclassified at n=15.** The 9-paper file used a two-way split (standalone Discussion vs. merged Results-and-discussion). The 6 new papers introduce two further structural types that do not fit either category cleanly:
- **Standalone Discussion:** 11/15 — the original 7 (Yuen, Nguyen 2025, Chounna Yemele, Zhu, Ca Mau, Rodrigues do Amaral, Luu) plus 4 new (Strozzi, Bendoni, Dini, Barthelemy).
- **Merged Results-and-discussion WITH a separate, later Conclusions section:** 2/15 — Shahi, Li (both from the original 9; no new paper matches this exact structure).
- **Discussion and Conclusion fully fused into one terminal section** (new category): 1/15 — Cavalié et al. Distinct from the merged-Results-and-discussion papers because here it is Discussion+Conclusion that fuse, not Results+Discussion, and nothing follows except back-matter.
- **No Discussion-equivalent section at all**, with interpretive/limitation content split between a Results-section tail and a Conclusions section (new category): 1/15 — Martins et al.

Any claim in this Step (or Step 4) about *where in the Discussion* a move occurs, or about *what the Discussion's terminal content is*, is tested against the **11-paper standalone-Discussion base specifically**, not against all 15 — exactly the discipline that exposed A4b's breakage at n=9, now carried forward deliberately rather than let slip as the corpus grew messier.

### A. Recurring patterns (re-tested against all 15 papers)

**A1. Concordance-first validation: a paper's own headline finding is checked against an independent prior published measurement/result, using explicit agreement-language.**
- All 7 conforming instances from the 9-paper file stand unchanged (Shahi, Nguyen 2025, Chounna Yemele, Li, Zhu, Amaral, Luu).
- New conforming instances: Bendoni §7 Para 4 ("This finding is consistent with the conclusions of... the IPCC Sixth Assessment Report (IPCC, 2023)," directly quoted) and the Oarsoaldea passage ("consistent with the findings of Vousdoukas et al., 2017"); Dini et al. §5.2 Para 1 ("similar to the final acceleration stage detected with optical images by Lacroix et al., 2023"), repeated and reinforced at §5.3 Para 3 and §5.5 Para 1 (a genuinely cross-method form of A1 — InSAR-derived coherence validated against an independently, optically derived timing estimate for the *same landslide*, not a different site); Barthelemy et al. §4.3 Para 2 ("have been widely documented in the literature, see for example Buras et al., 2020," validating the index's face validity against known 2003/2018 drought years).
- Non-conforming: Yuen (design exclusion, unchanged), Cavalié (its central comparison is a self-citation to its own earlier, narrower-window study — a comparative-methodology use, not an independent external validation of this paper's own new finding), Strozzi and Martins (perform the analogous move internally, tracked as D1 below, not counted here).
- **Raw count: 10/15.** **Adjusted count: 10/14** with Yuen excluded for design reasons (unchanged from n=9's logic). Both counts clear the two-thirds threshold cleanly for the first time (n=9's 7/9 raw did not clear it; only the adjusted 7/8 did) — **A1 strengthens** from "conditional on design, clears threshold only when adjusted" to "clears threshold on both raw and adjusted counts." The design-contingency qualification from the 9-paper file (A1 requires an independently-measurable own output) is retained and sharpened: Strozzi and Martins both *have* such an output, but validate it internally rather than externally — a genuinely different sub-case the original file could not see with only Yuen and Ca Mau as non-conforming examples.

**A2. Discordant/unexpected findings are named explicitly and reframed as informative rather than as errors.**
- All 7 conforming instances from the 9-paper file stand (Yuen, Shahi, Chounna Yemele, Li, Zhu, Amaral, and Nguyen 2025's weaker-marked version).
- New conforming instances: Strozzi §6.1 Para 4 (Brienz's lower-than-expected non-forested PS density, explicitly explained as a technique-ceiling effect) and §6.2 (the systematic C-band underestimation at all three velocity tiers, quantified rather than glossed); Bendoni §7 Para 4 (the apparent contradiction between a projected regional wave-height increase and Massa's near-zero local water-level change, explicitly named and resolved) and the Vilanova riverine exception (one scenario showing a decrease against an otherwise consistent increase pattern, stated within the same sentence rather than folded away); Dini et al. §5.3 Para 2 (the unreconciled 2-month vs. 5-month lead-time discrepancy against Jacquemart and Tiampo 2021, stated plainly without forced reconciliation) and §5.4.2 Para 2 (the InSAR-vs-optical acceleration-onset timing discordance, treated as evidence *for* the paper's own contribution rather than against it — flagged separately below as a candidate refinement); Barthelemy §4.3 Para 3 (the most extensively developed two-directional discordance-handling passage in the full corpus: drought-years-without-damage and damage-without-drought, both explained via the CatNat institutional mechanism).
- Non-conforming: Cavalié (no explicit named discordant/null finding in the merged Discussion-and-conclusion); Ca Mau and Luu remain non-conforming as in the original file; Martins performs a related but distinctly different move (see the new "discordance-as-supporting-evidence" pattern below) rather than a standard A2 instance.
- **Raw count: 12/15.** Clears two-thirds comfortably — **A2 strengthens**, from 7/9 (78%, already above threshold) to 12/15 (80%), essentially the same or marginally higher proportion, now on a much larger and more structurally diverse base (four different hazard/instrument domains represented among the new conforming papers).

**A3. Mechanisms proposed beyond what the paper's own data can test are explicitly attributed to the source, using reporting verbs, rather than adopted in the paper's own voice.**
- All 6 conforming instances from the 9-paper file stand (Nguyen 2025, Chounna Yemele, Yuen, Li, Zhu, Ca Mau).
- New conforming instances: Dini et al. — the single most citation-dense A3 paper in the corpus, with at least three distinct instances: §5.4.1 Para 1 (Agliardi et al. 2020's hydro-mechanical fluid-solid-coupling framework applied to a new landslide instance), §5.4.1 Para 2 (Lacroix et al. 2022's pore-pressure mechanism applied to explain an *absence* of an expected post-seismic effect — a distinctive variant, using an attributed mechanism to explain a negative result rather than a positive one), §5.4.2 Para 1 (a four-source mechanistic chain: Agliardi 2020 twice, Zhou 2018, Lacroix 2020, the densest single attributed-mechanism paragraph in the 15-paper corpus); Barthelemy — two instances: §4.2 Para 2 (Ravina 1983's topsoil hydraulic-conductivity mechanism, notable because it is directly cross-validated against the paper's *own* Fig. 7 data rather than substituting for data the paper cannot measure, a "confirmed, not substituted" A3 sub-case not clearly distinguished in the 9-paper file) and §4.4.6 (four separate citations — Freeman 1992, Hawkins 2013, Page 1998, Cooling and Ward 1948 — for un-tested local/building-level vulnerability factors, explicitly named as beyond this paper's own municipal-scale data).
- Non-conforming: Strozzi, Bendoni, Cavalié, Martins all argue their mechanisms directly from their own data/models rather than borrowing from literature to explain an unmeasured variable — consistent with the 9-paper file's qualification that A3 fires specifically when a mechanism concerns a variable the paper's own instrumentation did not measure.
- **Raw count: 8/15.** Below the two-thirds threshold (53%), roughly the same proportion as n=9 (6/9 = 67%, actually slightly higher than n=15's 53%) — **A3 weakens slightly in proportion** though the raw count grows. This is informative, not a defect: four of the six new papers (Strozzi, Bendoni, Cavalié, Martins) are exactly the kind of paper where the central mechanism sits fully within what the paper's own data/model directly shows (SAR-technique physics, hydrodynamic-model calculation, a creep-model fit, a satellite-vs-reanalysis comparison) — reinforcing, with a larger and more diverse sample, the 9-paper file's contingency claim that A3 depends on the paper needing to explain a consequence or driver lying *outside* its own measured scope, and further suggesting that method/instrument-focused papers (a genre well-represented in this batch of 6, less so in the original 9) are systematically less likely to need this move at all.

**A4a. Explicit limitations are placed locally, immediately adjacent to the specific claim they qualify, rather than only as a generic list.**
- All 8 conforming instances from the 9-paper file stand (all except Li).
- All 6 new papers conform, several with unusually extensive or systematic development: Strozzi (three instances: §6.1 Para 4 PSI velocity ceiling, §6.2 Para 2 Brienz post-2019 breakdown, §6.2 Para 3 phase-unwrapping-error attribution); Bendoni (five distinct, locally-placed limitations: single-RCM/ensemble choice, DEM resolution, wave-runup-formula uncertainty, compound-event non-interaction, tidal-regime stationarity); Dini et al. (three instances: sampling-interval limitation quantified to specific day-counts, 1/v-truncation limitation with an explicit can/cannot-show statement, image-resolution caveat named before its conclusion rather than after); Cavalié (a distinct, heavily hedged form — nearly an entire paragraph, Para 3, is a locally-placed, carefully tiered uncertainty-bounding passage on the single central open question, future creep trajectory); Barthelemy (the single most systematically organized limitations architecture in the full 15-paper corpus: six named, independently developed sub-limitations in §4.4, with explicit relative-severity self-ranking in two instances — a genuinely new level of rigor not seen in the original 9 or the other 5 new papers); Martins (two instances, one unusually located in the tail of the Results section rather than the Discussion-equivalent content, itself informative for how loosely "the Discussion" can be bounded when no section is so named).
- **Raw count: 14/15** (all except Li). This is now, unambiguously, the single strongest, most confidently generalizable pattern in the entire framework — up from 8/9 (89%) to 14/15 (93%), and the only exception (Li) remains explicable by the same structural cause identified at n=9 (its merged-section format pushes limitation content entirely into a separate Conclusions section, a structural rather than rhetorical deviation). **A4a strengthens further and is now the anchor pattern for the Step 5 "high-confidence NHESS-acceptable convention" claim.**

**A4b. Limitations are never the terminal content of the Discussion — substantive synthesis or implication content always follows the last limitation.**
Re-tested strictly against the 11-paper standalone-Discussion base (7 original + 4 new: Strozzi, Bendoni, Dini, Barthelemy), because this is a position claim and the merged/fused/absent-Discussion papers (Shahi, Li, Cavalié, Martins) cannot be scored on "is the Discussion's last paragraph a limitation" in the same sense.
- Among the original 7 standalone papers: 4 ended non-terminally (affirmative synthesis, forward-research call, or a hybrid), 3 ended directly on an unresolved limitation (Amaral, Luu, and functionally Ca Mau) — as already established and retracted at n=9.
- Among the 4 new standalone papers: Strozzi ends on §6.3 Para 5, a forward-looking/affirmative synthesis (non-terminal-limitation); Dini et al. ends on §5.5 Para 2, a hybrid of forward-research-call and complementarity-framing (non-terminal-limitation); Barthelemy ends on §4.5 Para 3, an affirmative, explicitly conditioned generalizability close (non-terminal-limitation); **Bendoni ends on the tidal-regime closing reflection** ("On the one hand, we are making a strong assumption... On the other hand, the knowledge of specific characteristics of the analyzed area are crucial") — a genuinely terminal, unresolved methodological limitation with no subsequent synthesis, joining Amaral, Luu, and Ca Mau as a fourth clear instance of a Discussion ending directly on a limitation.
- **Combined standalone base: 7 of 11 papers end non-terminally (64%), 4 of 11 end directly on an unresolved limitation (36%).** This is essentially the same proportion as the original 7-paper base (4/7 = 57% non-terminal), not a meaningfully different ratio — **A4b's retraction is CONFIRMED, not reversed or partially recovered, at n=15.** The larger, more diverse sample does not rescue the claim that Discussions "always" or "usually" close affirmatively; if anything, the consistency of the ~35-40% terminal-limitation rate across two independently-sampled sub-corpora (the original 9 and this batch of 6) makes the retraction more, not less, confident — this looks like a genuine, stable minority convention (roughly one Discussion in three legitimately ends on an honestly stated, unresolved limitation) rather than an artifact of which papers happened to be sampled first.

**A5. Practical/policy implications are drawn directly and explicitly from findings that involve human behavior, decision-relevant quantities, or infrastructure/safety consequences, scoped to match what the finding can support.**
- All 7 conforming instances from the 9-paper file stand.
- New conforming instances, both reinforcing the register-calibration qualification already noted at n=9: Cavalié §"Discussion and conclusion" Para 4 — a generalized, stakeholder-category-level implication ("will inform managers and policymakers in identifying zones with exposure to hazards"), explicitly NOT site-specific or quantitatively prescriptive, directly because Para 3 has just stated the future creep trajectory is unresolved — a clean confirmation that implication register tracks certainty; Barthelemy §4.5 Para 2 — the most directly actionable, specifically addressed implication of the new batch, naming two distinct stakeholder categories (French monitoring institutions; the insurance industry, with a named existing model — Charpentier et al. 2022 — to integrate into); Martins §4 Para 4 — a genuinely new implication *register* not seen anywhere in the 9-paper file or the rest of this batch: a data-adoption/research-infrastructure implication ("fostering their usage for all sorts of climate applications, thus increasing user uptake of these datasets"), addressed to the scientific-data-user community rather than a hazard-management stakeholder.
- Non-conforming, both for the same reason already established as A5's core contingency: Strozzi and Dini et al. both produce methodological/technological or research-agenda-level implications rather than policy/practice ones, and Dini et al.'s own Discussion explicitly states why (generalizability to unknown-location detection is an open research question, not yet an actionable capability); Bendoni's implications are methodological/scope-bounding rather than prescriptive.
- **Raw count: 10/15.** Clears two-thirds. **A5 strengthens marginally in raw count (7/9 to 10/15, both ~67-78%)** but more importantly, **the register-calibration qualification is no longer a caveat on A5 — it is now a load-bearing, four-times-independently-confirmed part of the pattern itself** (Amaral and Luu at n=9; Strozzi, Dini, and Cavalié's generalized form at n=15). A5 should now be stated as: practical implications are drawn when the evidentiary basis and the finding's actionability support them, calibrated in both strength AND register (hazard-management-prescriptive vs. methodological vs. research-agenda vs., newly, data-infrastructure/adoption-oriented) — not as "papers usually produce a policy implication, with occasional exceptions."

### B. Paper-specific / topic-specific / design-specific patterns, re-tested at n=15

**B1. Synthesis-by-chaining across pre-established causal links, opening a Discussion by consolidating a multi-part Results section before interpreting it.** Still 2/9 at the prior count (Yuen, Ca Mau); no clear new instance among the 6 new papers (none of them opens by aggregatively restating a large multi-part Results section in this specific way — Strozzi's §6.1 Para 1 comes closest but is a narrower, single-topic orienting move, not a full-Results consolidation). Remains 2/15, below threshold, unchanged.

**B2. Dual literature use of the same citation for both concordance and discordance within one paragraph.** No longer a single-paper (Shahi-only) artifact: Dini et al. deploys this move repeatedly and extensively — Jacquemart and Tiampo (2021) is used for concordance (general early-indicator validity, §5.3 Para 2 and §5.5 Para 1) AND for an unreconciled discordance (lead-time magnitude, §5.3 Para 2, same paragraph) in the SAME citation; Lacroix et al. (2023) is used across §5.2 (concordance, 3-month critical-window timing) and §5.4.2 Para 2 (discordance, acceleration-onset timing) — a citation reused across different paragraphs of the SAME Discussion for genuinely distinct concordant/discordant purposes, a broader-scope version of Shahi's single-paragraph dual use. Barthelemy also shows a milder instance: Charpentier et al. (2022) is used in §4.1 for a methodological-comparison/discordance point and in §4.5 for a practical-integration-target purpose. **Raw count: 3/15** (Shahi, Dini, Barthelemy) — still below threshold but no longer a true one-off; the underlying discipline (a single well-chosen close comparator study can legitimately be reused for multiple distinct rhetorical purposes across a Discussion) is now evidenced across three structurally different paper types.

**B3. Merged/fused/absent Discussion-section structures change what "the Discussion" even is.** Substantially more varied at n=15 than the binary the 9-paper file described. As detailed in the position-testability base above, there are now four structural categories, not two: standalone (11), merged-with-separate-Conclusions (2: Shahi, Li), Discussion-and-Conclusion fully fused (1: Cavalié), and no Discussion-equivalent section at all (1: Martins). Cavalié's case sharpens the original B3 observation: like Li, its limitation content survives inside the merged section (Para 3's extensive uncertainty-bounding) rather than being pushed out entirely — but unlike either Shahi or Li, there is no later, separate Conclusions section to push content INTO, meaning the merged section must simultaneously perform both closing-Discussion-move and formal-Conclusion functions. Martins's case is the most structurally novel in the corpus: its interpretive/limitation content is not merely relocated (as in the merged-section papers) but *split* — a genuine limitation lives in the tail of the Results section, and the bulk of the Discussion-type work (mechanism explanation, concordance/discordance handling, a circularity check) lives in a Conclusions section never labeled "Discussion" in any form. This strongly suggests B3 is not really one pattern but a family of related structural adaptations, best treated in Step 5/6 as "the Discussion's rhetorical work is always present in some form across a hazard-science paper, but where it is textually located is genuinely variable and paper-specific, not predictable from section headings alone" — a stronger, more general version of the original B3 observation.

**B4. Optimization-algorithm mechanistic exposition, citation-dense, algorithm-by-algorithm.** Unchanged — no analogous passage in any of the 6 new papers. Remains 1/15.

**B5. Two-tiered (or multi-tiered) hedging within a single mechanistic claim.** Gains further support: Cavalié §"Discussion and conclusion" Para 3 hedges an entire chain of candidate creep-trigger mechanisms (platform loading, precipitation-driven fluid pressure, earthquake shaking) each with its own explicit hedge word ("can," "could also," "may occur"), while distinguishing the one unhedged, established fact (creep is presently occurring) from the multiply-hedged downstream consequences — one of the most systematically and consistently multi-tiered-hedged single paragraphs in the full corpus; Dini et al. §5.4.1 Para 2 doubly hedges its pore-pressure-migration explanation for a cross-site (Achoma-vs-Maca) discordance. Now 4/15 (Chounna Yemele, Amaral, Cavalié, Dini) — still below threshold, but the underlying discipline (calibrate hedge strength independently for each candidate mechanism within one paragraph, not uniformly for the whole paragraph) is confirmed across four structurally very different paper types (InSAR subsidence attribution, single-event hydrology, InSAR creep modeling, InSAR landslide precursors) and is generalized into Step 5/6 as a design principle, as it was at n=9.

**B6. Cross-regional/multi-source literature triangulation to support a single claim.** Gains a third instance: Strozzi §6.2 Para 3 uses two independent external citations (Ng et al. 2012, Nishiguchi et al. 2017) to calibrate expectations for a specific quantitative comparison, though this is a lighter-weight instance than Chounna Yemele's or Amaral's. More substantively, Dini et al.'s §5.4.1 Para 2 mechanism chain draws on four distinct sources (Agliardi 2020 x2, Zhou 2018, Lacroix 2020) for one mechanistic claim — technically an A3 instance but also a dense multi-source triangulation in the B6 sense. Now best described as 3-4/15 depending on how strictly "triangulation to support ONE claim" is separated from ordinary multi-citation A3 support; remains below threshold but the practice is now confirmed across five structurally distinct paper types (up from two at n=9), reinforcing that it is a real, repeatable move available to any Discussion needing to support an unusually load-bearing single claim, not a topic-specific quirk.

**New pattern — C1. Rival-hypothesis elimination or counter-argument neutralization before the paper commits to its preferred explanation.**
- The 4 original instances stand (Zhu, Li, Ca Mau, Amaral).
- Dini et al. is, by a wide margin, the single richest source of this pattern in the entire 15-paper corpus, with FOUR independent instances in one Discussion: §5.2 Para 1 (land-cover changes eliminated as the cause of coherence loss, deformation preferred); §5.4.1 Para 2 (seismicity-alone and rainfall-alone both eliminated via specific counter-evidence, a combined-factor explanation preferred, with an explicit, quantified cross-site comparator — the Maca landslide — used to sharpen rather than soften the elimination); §5.4.2 Para 2 (rainfall-magnitude-alone eliminated via a within-study year-to-year comparison); §5.4.2 Para 6 (river-toe erosion eliminated via an independent PlanetScope optical dataset, with the null result treated as informative confirmation of the paper's preferred progressive-internal-failure account).
- No new instance in Strozzi, Bendoni, Cavalié, Barthelemy (Barthelemy's circularity-check evaluates and declines an *alternative method*, a related but distinct move — kept separate, see below), or Martins.
- **Raw count by paper: 5/15** (Zhu, Li, Ca Mau, Amaral, Dini). Below the 10/15 recurring threshold in terms of how many PAPERS use it, but the total instance-count across the corpus is now 8 (4 original + 4 in Dini alone) — the single paper with the most process-attribution-heavy, single-case mechanistic-reconstruction design (Dini et al.) essentially demonstrates that C1 is a natural, even default, move for that specific kind of paper (a hazard-precursor reconstruction needing to rule out multiple plausible triggers for one event), while remaining rare in paper types (model-comparison, scenario-projection, satellite-vs-reanalysis validation) where there is no comparably strong single rival explanation to name and reject. **C1 does not cross the recurring threshold at n=15 (5/15 papers, 33%), but is now much better evidenced as a genuine, design-contingent move** (contingent on a single-event or single-mechanism attribution design with multiple plausible candidate causes) rather than a marginal curiosity — a clear case for retention as a named, high-value paragraph type in Step 5/6 without inflating it to "recurring."

**New pattern — C2. Limitation-then-workaround: a data or method gap is named and immediately met with a substitute source, proxy variable, or design choice that partially compensates, rather than left as an open gap.**
- The 5 original instances stand (Zhu, Ca Mau, Nguyen 2025, Yuen, Luu).
- New instances: Strozzi — three within one Discussion (§6.1 Para 4, PSI ceiling met with DInSAR/stacking as an alternative technique; §6.2 Para 2, satellite-PSI failure at Brienz's fastest sectors met by naming the terrestrial GLSAR/GNSS network as the substitute data source; §6.3 Para 3, the PSI velocity-range gap explicitly paired with SBAS as the compensating technique); Bendoni — Para 2 (the single-RCM/no-ensemble choice paired with bootstrap confidence intervals as a partial compensation, with an unusually candid admission that the workaround addresses "a different source of uncertainty," not a full fix — arguably the most honest single instance of this move in the corpus, since most C2 instances do not explicitly flag the workaround's own incompleteness); Barthelemy — §4.4.3 (the annual/daily temporal-resolution mismatch paired with an explicit argument for why the finer daily data partially compensates, itself paired with a named residual uncertainty that survives the compensation — a nuanced middle case between "fully compensated" and "left fully open").
- **Raw count: 8/15.** Clears past the original 5/9 (56%) proportionally to 8/15 (53%), essentially flat as a proportion but now confirmed across five structurally distinct designs (InSAR attribution, remote-sensing feature engineering, scenario hydrodynamics, InSAR technique comparison, multi-model climate scenario projection, LSM-based index calibration). Still below the two-thirds recurring threshold; retained in Step 5/6 as a well-evidenced refinement of A4a, with Bendoni's candid "different source of uncertainty" admission and Barthelemy's residual-uncertainty pairing offered as the corpus's clearest models of how to state a workaround's own limits honestly rather than implying it fully resolves the gap.

**New pattern — C3. Explicit two-tier statement of what a piece of evidence can and cannot demonstrate, used to bound a claim precisely.**
- The 4 original instances stand (Chounna Yemele, Li, Amaral, Luu).
- New instances: Dini et al. §5.4.2 Para 5 — a textbook instance: "These inverse velocity plots are therefore not intended to predict failure timing but to illustrate the changing kinematic behaviour across acceleration phases," a precise, self-imposed boundary on the paper's own central diagnostic tool, pre-empting overclaiming elsewhere in the same Discussion; Barthelemy §4.4.5 — an explicit relative-severity self-ranking ("At this stage, we are not trying to make precise damage predictions but rather only identify drought years. Therefore, this source of uncertainty is not the most dominant"), a variant that bounds a claim not against an absolute standard but against the paper's own stated scope; Martins §4 Para 5 — another textbook instance: the satellite record cannot yet support 30-year climate normals, but already supports decadal-variability and cross-dataset-diagnostic work, an explicit and cleanly stated two-tier claim closing the paper's Discussion-equivalent content.
- **Raw count: 7/15.** Nearly doubles the original 4/9 in raw terms and holds essentially flat in proportion (44% at n=9, 47% at n=15) — still below the two-thirds threshold but confirmed as a genuinely repeatable, high-value move across an even wider range of evidence types than the original file documented (satellite gravimetry, a validation statistic, a derived hydraulic quantity, administrative damage records, an inverse-velocity kinematic diagnostic, a relative-severity uncertainty ranking, a satellite data record's temporal sufficiency) — retained and reinforced in Step 5/6 as a distinct, nameable discipline, distinguishable from a plain limitation statement by its explicit can-show/cannot-show or can-show-X/cannot-show-Y structure.
