**Genuinely new pattern, visible only at n=15 — D1. Internal cross-instrument/cross-dataset validation, structurally parallel to A1 but performed WITHIN the paper rather than against external literature.**
- Strozzi §6.2, all three paragraphs: satellite InSAR (PSI, SBAS) validated at each of three sites against independent terrestrial monitoring networks (GNSS, GB-DInSAR, MTS, car-borne GLSAR) that the paper itself operated or accessed — an unusually thorough, quantified, velocity-stratified validation exercise (explicit percent-recovery figures at different velocity regimes) that performs exactly the evidentiary function A1 performs (does the paper's headline measurement match an independent ground truth?) without a single external published comparator study being the source of that ground truth.
- Martins §4 Para 4: LSTMax-based diagnostics validated against ERA5-Land's SKTMax-based diagnostics, with agreement treated as validating and (more distinctively) well-explained disagreement ALSO treated as validating (see the next new pattern).
- **Raw count: 2/15.** Below threshold, but flagged explicitly because it is evidentially just as rigorous as A1 in both instances — the distinguishing feature is simply where the comparator data originates (within vs. outside the paper), not the strength of the validation logic. This matters directly for Step 5: a paper without an obvious external prior-literature comparator for its headline output should not conclude no concordance-style validation is possible — an internal, independently operated comparator dataset (a different sensor, a different model, ground-truth instrumentation) can perform the same function.

**Genuinely new pattern, visible only at n=15 — D2. Discordance actively converted into supporting evidence for the preferred data source or explanation, not merely explained and set aside.**
- Martins §4 Para 4 is the clearest, most explicit instance in the corpus: "the comparisons of LSTMax- and SKTMax-based metrics further reinforced the confidence of the former, not only because they compare relatively well in general but also because when they do not, there are plausible reasons for it" — an explicit, stated epistemic principle (a well-explained disagreement is itself evidence of validity, because the comparator's failure mode is independently predictable from a known structural limitation of that specific comparator).
- This is a genuinely stronger move than A2 (name a discordance, reframe it as informative) — A2 stops at "this discordance does not undermine the finding and teaches us something about heterogeneity"; D2 goes further, to "this discordance actively increases confidence in the preferred result." Dini et al.'s §5.4.2 Para 2 (InSAR detecting the critical transition earlier than an independent optical method) is a partial cousin — the discordance is framed as supporting the paper's own novelty claim rather than merely being tolerated — but it does not carry the explicit, stated epistemic principle Martins articulates, and is scored as A2 (favorable-novelty-supporting discordance) rather than a clean D2 instance.
- **Raw count: 1/15 clean instance (Martins), 1/15 close cousin (Dini).** Far below any recurring threshold, but flagged explicitly in Step 5/6 as a genuinely novel, high-value rhetorical move worth naming for its own sake — directly relevant to any paper (including a nowcasting model validated against imperfectly independent target data) that needs to argue a partial mismatch between two data sources strengthens, rather than weakens, the case for the preferred one.

**Genuinely new pattern, visible only at n=15 — D3. Explicit circularity check on the paper's own validation logic: naming a potential circularity between predictor and validation target, and resolving it either by argument (found real but weak) or by demonstration (checked and found not to apply).**
- Barthelemy §4.4.2: the clearest, most extensively reasoned instance in the corpus — a real structural circularity (the drought index and the CatNat decree's meteorological criterion both derive from the ISBA model) is named directly, argued to be weak on a specific, checkable ground (different, repeatedly modified model version), and a genuine alternative validation method (InSAR/EGMS) is evaluated in detail and explicitly declined with three distinct, stated reasons (interpretive complexity, unavailability at the time, insufficient temporal overlap).
- Martins §4 Para 3: the "checked and cleared" counterpart — SWI is an input to the LST retrieval algorithm's cloudy-sky pathway, which could threaten the independence of the paper's own drought-vegetation-stress interpretation, but the paper demonstrates this circularity does not apply under the conditions being studied (heatwave conditions predominantly use the clear-sky retrieval pathway, which does not depend on SWI).
- **Raw count: 2/15.** Below threshold, but flagged explicitly because this is a distinct, higher-order discipline than an ordinary limitation statement — it requires the paper to interrogate whether its OWN validation logic is doing genuine independent work, not merely to name an external data gap. Both instances model exemplary practice: naming the concern before a reviewer would, and resolving it either through reasoned argument or direct demonstration rather than through silence or a vague disclaimer.

---

## STEP 4 — Derived Argumentative Sequence (actual, not textbook-imposed; re-derived across 15 papers)

The naive textbook template remains: **finding → interpretation → literature comparison → agreement/disagreement explanation → implication → limitations (at the end) → conclusion.**

What is actually observed, paragraph by paragraph, across all fifteen papers, updating the 9-paper sequence:

1. **Orienting/framing paragraph(s).** Unchanged in kind — re-establish stakes, sometimes with no specific finding yet, sometimes as an aggregative restatement of a multi-part Results section (B1, now 2/15). New at n=15: a paper's very first Discussion-equivalent content can name a genuine, foundational open question as its organizing premise rather than a settled finding — Cavalié's Para 3 (the largest single paragraph in that paper's merged section) is built entirely around the explicit statement "its future evolution is uncertain and still at stake," with every subsequent sentence hedged accordingly; this is a variant of Rodrigues do Amaral's opening-paragraph limitation-as-framing device (already noted at n=9), now confirmed in a second, structurally different paper (creep-model fitting vs. single-event hydrology).
2. **Concordance-check paragraph**, present for 10/15 papers on the strict external-literature definition (A1), now supplemented by an internal-cross-instrument-validation variant (D1, 2/15: Strozzi, Martins) for papers whose comparator dataset originates within the paper rather than in prior published literature. Both forms perform the same evidentiary function; which form is available depends on whether the paper's own design includes an independently operated comparator instrument/model (satellite-vs-ground-truth; satellite-vs-reanalysis) or must rely on prior published measurements of a comparable quantity elsewhere.
3. **Rival-hypothesis elimination or counter-argument neutralization** (C1, now 5/15 papers, 8 total instances given Dini et al.'s four), most visible and dense in single-event/single-case mechanistic-attribution designs (Dini et al.'s Achoma reconstruction is the clearest exemplar in the full corpus) — confirmed at n=15 as design-contingent rather than universal, exactly as flagged at n=9, now with a much richer illustrative case.
4. **Explicit circularity check on the paper's own validation logic** (D3, new at n=15, 2/15) — a genuinely new step in the sequence, appearing when a paper's predictor and validation target share a structural dependency (a common underlying model, a common input variable) that a careful reader would otherwise flag; both instances (Barthelemy, Martins) place this check adjacent to the concordance/validation content it qualifies, consistent with the local-placement discipline already established for ordinary limitations (A4a).
5. **Mechanistic elaboration**, single- or multi-tiered (B5, now 4/15, richer evidence base), attributing beyond-own-data mechanisms to literature (A3, 8/15) specifically when the mechanism concerns an unmeasured variable — confirmed at n=15 as strongly design-contingent: instrument/method-comparison and scenario-modeling papers (a genre well-represented in this batch) rarely need it, since their central mechanisms sit inside what their own data/model directly shows.
6. **Discordant/unexpected-finding handling**, inserted at the point the relevant comparison arises (A2, 12/15, the strongest raw count of any comparative-handling pattern in the framework), often productively reframed rather than merely acknowledged. New at n=15: a discordance can be pushed one step further than reframing, into **active conversion to supporting evidence** for the preferred result (D2, Martins' explicit statement of this epistemic principle) — a genuinely new terminal point on the discordance-handling spectrum, beyond "acknowledge and explain" (the modal case) and even beyond "reframe as informative about heterogeneity" (Shahi, Zhu).
7. **Limitations**, interleaved at the point of local relevance (A4a, now 14/15 — the single strongest, most confidently generalizable pattern in the entire 15-paper framework). **Confirmed at n=15: limitations are NOT reliably followed by further substantive content when tested against the correct 11-paper standalone-Discussion base** — 4/11 standalone papers (Amaral, Luu, Ca Mau, and now Bendoni) end their Discussion directly on an unresolved limitation, essentially the same ~35-40% rate observed at both the original 7-paper base and this batch's 4-paper addition. The generalization stands exactly as stated at n=9, now on a stronger evidential footing: limitations are placed locally wherever they qualify a specific claim (near-universal, 93% of the full corpus), but whether the Discussion's very last paragraph is a limitation, an implication, or a synthesis is genuinely paper-specific, and the ~35-40% terminal-limitation rate looks stable rather than sample-dependent.
8. **Practical/policy implication**, sometimes bundled with the finding's own paragraph, sometimes deferred (A5, 10/15). Confirmed and strengthened at n=15: implication *register* (prescriptive-for-hazard-management vs. generalized-stakeholder-category vs. methodological/technological vs. research-agenda vs., newly, data-adoption/research-infrastructure) tracks the finding's actionability and certainty as tightly and predictably as implication *presence/strength* does — this is now a core, load-bearing part of how A5 should be described, not a secondary caveat.
9. **A final closing move**, distinct from the formal Conclusion section (where one exists separately). Confirmed at n=15, with the position-testability base corrected to 11 standalone papers: three closing-move types remain all attested at meaningful frequency — (a) reassertion of the paper's broader method/contribution/generalized policy relevance (Yuen, Nguyen 2025, Li, and now Strozzi's multi-frequency-integration close, Barthelemy's conditioned-generalizability close, and — notably, despite being a fused rather than standalone Discussion — Cavalié's climate-change/policy-relevance broadening close); (b) explicit forward-research call (Chounna Yemele, Zhu, Luu, and now Dini et al.'s complementarity-and-generalizability close, a hybrid of (a) and (b)); (c) an unresolved methodological caution with no synthesis attached (Rodrigues do Amaral, Ca Mau, and now Bendoni's two-sided tidal-stationarity reflection, the fourth clear standalone instance).

**Key deviations from the naive template, explicitly flagged (updated at n=15):**
- Limitations placed locally throughout is confirmed and strengthened further (14/15, 93%) — the single most robust finding across both rounds of this analysis.
- Limitations being non-terminal remains retracted, now with a second, independently-sampled 4-paper standalone sub-base (Strozzi, Bendoni, Dini, Barthelemy) landing at almost exactly the same ~35-40% terminal-limitation rate as the original 7-paper base — the retraction is not just confirmed but strengthened into a stable, cross-sample finding rather than a single-corpus artifact.
- Discordant findings continue to be actively repurposed as evidence for a broader claim (Barthelemy's institutional-mechanism explanation of a two-directional mismatch; Dini et al.'s favorable framing of an InSAR-vs-optical timing discordance), and this batch reveals discordance-handling can go one step further still into active conversion-to-supporting-evidence (D2, Martins) — the naive template's silence on discordance handling entirely is even less adequate at n=15 than at n=9.
- Concordance-checking is confirmed as contingent on an independently-measurable own output, and this batch reveals a genuine sub-case: the independent comparator can be internal (a different instrument/model within the same paper, D1) rather than external published literature, without weakening the evidentiary function the check performs.
- A genuinely new step (explicit circularity-checking, D3) appears in the sequence for the first time at n=15, specific to papers whose predictor and validation target share a structural dependency — not visible at all in the original 9-paper file, where no paper's design created this specific risk.
- Rival-hypothesis elimination (C1) is confirmed as a real, design-contingent move, now dramatically better evidenced by a single paper (Dini et al., 4 instances) that happens to have exactly the single-event, multi-plausible-cause design where this move is most natural — reinforcing that its absence from a paper's Discussion reflects design fit, not a missed rhetorical opportunity.

---

## STEP 5 — Reusable Discussion Framework (topic-agnostic template, grounded in 15 papers)

### Prefatory note: which design principles are now high-confidence NHESS-acceptable conventions, and which remain genuinely design-contingent

Given the explicit purpose of this analysis — finding patterns robust across accepted NHESS papers as the strongest available signal for what NHESS reviewers and editors find acceptable — this update distinguishes two tiers plainly, using the n=15 fractions:

**High-confidence, near-universal conventions (≥12/15, ≥80%), best treated as close to a house rule rather than a stylistic option:**
- **Local limitation placement (A4a, 14/15, 93%).** A limitation belongs immediately next to the specific claim it qualifies. This is now the single most robustly evidenced finding in the entire two-round analysis (8/9 at n=9, 14/15 at n=15, the only strengthening pattern that improved on both raw and adjusted counts every time it was re-tested). A manuscript that batches all its limitations into one generic paragraph at the very end is deviating from what 14 of 15 published, accepted NHESS papers actually do.
- **Discordant/unexpected findings are named and reframed, never suppressed (A2, 12/15, 80%).** A result that conflicts with prior literature, a stated hypothesis, or another part of the same paper's own results should be stated plainly, not minimized or omitted.

**Strongly evidenced, clears two-thirds, but with a stated contingency that must also be honored (10/15, 67%):**
- **Concordance-first validation (A1, 10/15 raw, 10/14 adjusted) — contingent on the study design producing an independently-measurable headline output**, and satisfiable either by an external published comparator OR (per D1, below threshold but evidentially equal in rigor) an internal, independently operated comparator instrument or model within the same paper. A design without either kind of comparator (a pure scenario-projection or integration-only study) legitimately has no concordance paragraph — forcing one would not make the manuscript more NHESS-like.
- **Practical/theoretical implications, calibrated in both strength and register to the finding's actionability and certainty (A5, 10/15) — the register-calibration qualification is now load-bearing, not a caveat.** A methodological, research-agenda, or data-adoption implication is not a weaker version of a policy recommendation; it is the correct move when the finding does not bear on an actionable, decision-relevant quantity.

**Genuinely contingent, well below the recurring threshold but reusable and worth deploying when the situation calls for it — NOT universal rules, and a manuscript should not force these where they do not fit:**
- Attributed-mechanism hedging (A3, 8/15) — needed only when a proposed mechanism concerns a variable the paper's own data/model did not measure.
- Rival-hypothesis elimination (C1, 5/15 papers, 8 instances) — most natural in single-event or single-case attribution designs with multiple plausible causes; largely absent from, and not missed in, comparison/scenario/validation-focused papers.
- Limitation-then-workaround (C2, 8/15), two-tier can-show/cannot-show bounding (C3, 7/15), multi-source literature triangulation (B6, 3-4/15), explicit circularity-checking (D3, 2/15), internal cross-instrument validation (D1, 2/15), and discordance-converted-to-supporting-evidence (D2, 1-2/15) — all real, repeatable, high-value moves, none yet a majority convention, each best selected because a specific situation calls for it (a genuine data/method gap; a genuinely ambiguous piece of evidence; multiple independent sources converging on one claim; a genuine shared-model circularity risk; an internally available comparator dataset; a well-explained disagreement that can be turned into positive evidence) rather than inserted by default.

**A firmly retracted claim, now confirmed retracted on a second, independent sample:**
- **A Discussion must end on an affirmative synthesis (A4b).** Roughly one Discussion in three (4/11 standalone papers at n=15, materially the same ratio as 3/7 at n=9) legitimately and honestly ends directly on an unresolved, precisely scoped limitation. This is not a defect to correct in a draft; it is a documented, stable minority convention.

### Design principles derived from Steps 1–4 (updated, integrating all 15 papers)

1. **Every Discussion paragraph should be traceable to exactly one specific finding** from the Results section. (14/15 support at n=15 via A4a's local-placement discipline, which presupposes this; unchanged in substance from the 9-paper file.)
2. **The order of moves within a paragraph is: name the finding → state what kind of claim you are about to make → make the claim at the certainty level the study design actually supports → state what follows from it, if anything.** Six claim-types are now attested: validated-by-external-precedent (A1), validated-by-internal-comparator (D1), newly-mechanistic-from-own-data, attributed-to-others (A3), rival-hypothesis-eliminated (C1), and genuinely-uncertain. A seventh, narrower move sits alongside these rather than replacing any of them: an explicit circularity check (D3) on whether the validation itself is doing independent work, performed before or alongside a concordance claim when the predictor and validation target share a structural dependency.
3. **Certainty language must match what the study design can support.** Unchanged in substance; the attribution move (A3) is confirmed, at n=15, to be strongly contingent on the mechanism concerning an unmeasured variable — instrument/method-comparison and scenario-modeling designs, well-represented in this batch, systematically need this move less often, not because they are less careful, but because their central claims sit inside their own directly demonstrated data.
4. **Limitations are placed where they qualify a specific claim, not batched at the end** (now the framework's single best-evidenced rule, 14/15). **A limitation is strongest when paired with an explicit statement of what compensates for it, OR — where the compensation is itself imperfect — an honest statement of what the workaround does and does not fix** (Bendoni's "although this is a different source of uncertainty" is the corpus's clearest model of this honesty). **Do not treat "the Discussion must not end on a limitation" as a rule** — roughly a third of accepted NHESS Discussions legitimately do.
5. **Discordant, null, or unexpected results are named explicitly and treated as an opportunity to refine the claim's scope** — and, in the strongest instances now documented at n=15, can be actively converted into supporting evidence for the preferred result or explanation when the discordant comparator's own failure mode is independently predictable from a known structural limitation of that comparator (D2). This is a distinct, higher-value move than mere reframing, worth reaching for specifically when a paper has a well-understood reason why an imperfect comparator diverges.
6. **Concordance with prior evidence functions as validation, not padding — and the comparator can legitimately be internal (a different instrument, model, or dataset within the same paper) as well as external published literature**, provided it is independently operated/derived from the paper's own primary result. A design with neither kind of available comparator (scenario-projection, integration-only) may legitimately have no concordance paragraph.
7. **Practical or theoretical implications should be scoped to the actual evidentiary weight of the finding AND to whether the finding bears on a decision-relevant, actionable quantity — and the implication's REGISTER, not only its strength, should be calibrated accordingly.** A hazard-management-prescriptive register, a generalized-stakeholder-category register, a methodological/technological register, a research-agenda register, and a data-adoption/research-infrastructure register are all correct choices for the right kind of finding — none is a fallback for a "weaker" paper.
8. **A rival explanation, or a factor that could weaken the paper's own central claim, is stronger when raised and addressed by the author than when left for a reader to raise** — and this move is most natural, and most valuable to deploy deliberately, in single-event or single-case attribution designs with multiple genuinely plausible causes; a comparison/validation/scenario-modeling design will often have no comparably strong rival to eliminate, and should not manufacture one.
9. **When a paper's predictor and its own validation target share a structural dependency (a common underlying model, a shared input variable), check explicitly whether this compromises the validation's independence, and resolve the check either by reasoned argument (why the dependency is likely weak) or by direct demonstration (why the dependency does not apply under the conditions studied) — do not leave a checkable circularity unaddressed for a reviewer to raise.** New at n=15; directly relevant to any nowcasting or forecasting model validated against a target that is not fully independent of the model's own inputs or of a related model version.

### Paragraph-type library (assemble in whatever order fits the actual findings — do not force a fixed count or sequence)

All eleven paragraph types from the 9-paper file's library remain valid and are carried forward unchanged in their core definitions (orienting; concordance; mechanistic; discordance/unexpected-result; null/negative-result; comparative-magnitude; boundary/heterogeneity; local limitation; genuinely unresolved; implication; closing). Three refinements and two additions from the 15-paper analysis:

- **Concordance paragraph, refined:** the comparator may be an external published prior result (the modal case) OR an internal, independently operated comparator dataset/instrument/model within the same paper — both satisfy the same evidentiary function. Omit entirely only when neither is available.
- **Rival-hypothesis-elimination paragraph, refined:** most natural, and most worth actively deploying, in a single-event or single-case attribution design where multiple plausible causes exist for one finding; less natural, and not to be forced, in a comparison/validation/scenario-modeling design.
- **Implication paragraph, refined:** the register (hazard-management-prescriptive, generalized-stakeholder-category, methodological/technological, research-agenda, or data-adoption/research-infrastructure) should be selected to match the finding's actionability and certainty — treat all five as equally legitimate outcomes of correct calibration, not a hierarchy from "strong" to "weak."
- **New — Circularity-check paragraph:** names a structural dependency between the paper's own predictor and its validation target (a shared underlying model, a shared input variable), and resolves it either by a reasoned argument for why the dependency is weak (citing a specific, checkable difference between the two uses) or by a direct demonstration that the dependency does not apply under the conditions studied. Place adjacent to the concordance/validation paragraph it qualifies.
- **New — Discordance-as-supporting-evidence paragraph:** takes a discordance between the paper's own preferred result and a comparator, and — where the comparator's specific failure mode is independently predictable from a known, named structural limitation of that comparator — states explicitly that the well-explained disagreement increases (not merely fails to undermine) confidence in the preferred result. Reserve for cases where the mechanism of the comparator's divergence is genuinely well understood and independently checkable; do not overuse this move to dismiss inconvenient comparisons.

### How to decide which paragraph types to use, and in what order, for a NEW study

Unchanged in its core logic from the 9-paper file, with two additions:
- Before writing a concordance paragraph, check whether the study design produces an independently-measurable headline output, AND whether the available comparator is external (published literature) or internal (a different instrument/model/dataset within the paper) — both are legitimate, neither should be manufactured where the design provides neither.
- Where the paper's own predictor and its validation target share any structural dependency (built from the same underlying model or a shared input), insert an explicit circularity-check paragraph before or alongside the concordance/validation content — resolve it by argument or demonstration, and place it where a careful reviewer would look for it.

---

## STEP 6 — Discussion Blueprint + Implicit Rules Checklist (updated, 15-paper support)

### Discussion Blueprint (generic, reusable skeleton)

The eleven-paragraph skeleton from the 9-paper file is retained with two paragraph types inserted (position given in relation to the existing paragraph numbers, not renumbering the whole skeleton, to keep the two versions comparable):

`Paragraph 1: [orienting / synthesis] → [restated central finding, or consolidation of a multi-part Results section] → [why this matters relative to the study's stated question] → [no new claim yet; may name a foundational design constraint OR a genuinely unresolved central open question as a framing device]`

`Paragraph 2 (only if the design supports it): [concordance check — external OR internal] → [central/headline finding] → [comparison against ≥1 specific prior result (named, published) OR an independently operated internal comparator dataset/instrument/model] → [state degree of match; treat match as validation, not filler] — [omit entirely for a design with neither kind of comparator]`

`Paragraph 2a (NEW, insert where a structural dependency exists between predictor and validation target): [circularity check] → [name the shared model/input] → [argue the dependency is weak on a specific, checkable ground, OR demonstrate the dependency does not apply under the conditions studied] → [state plainly what remains, if anything, unresolved by this check]`

`Paragraph 3 (as needed): [rival-hypothesis elimination] → [a plausible competing explanation for the central finding] → [a specific, checkable ground: magnitude mismatch, absence of expected evidence, testable prediction, within-study comparator case] → [report the outcome before committing to the preferred mechanism] — [most natural in single-event/single-case attribution designs; do not force in comparison/scenario designs]`

`Paragraph 4: [mechanistic] → [same or related finding] → [proposed explanation, tagged as demonstrated / literature-established / speculative, or multiple candidates each independently tagged] → [hedge language calibrated to each tag] — [needed specifically when the mechanism concerns a variable the paper's own data/model did not directly measure]`

`Paragraph 5: [discordance or null-result handling] → [a finding that conflicts with expectation, prior work, another part of this study, or an internal comparator] → [named conflict + candidate reason, OR reframing as evidence of heterogeneity, OR treated as informative confirmation, OR — where the comparator's divergence is independently well-explained — stated as ACTIVELY REINFORCING confidence in the preferred result] → [do not omit or minimize]`

`Paragraph 6: [boundary/heterogeneity] → [evidence that the central finding varies across sites/subgroups/conditions] → [explicit caution against over-generalizing] → [scope statement]`

`Paragraph 7: [local limitation] → [the specific claim being qualified] → [named weakness] → [precise statement of what is and is not affected] → [paired, where possible, with an explicit compensating move — and an honest statement of what the compensation does NOT fix, if it is imperfect]`

`Paragraph 8 (as needed): [genuinely unresolved anomaly] → [anomalous result] → [≥2 candidate explanations without forced resolution] → [explicit deferral to future work]`

`Paragraph 9: [comparative magnitude / broader context] → [central finding's scale] → [positioned against known comparable cases, an external design/regulatory standard, or a within-study alternative-configuration comparison] → [calibrates practical significance]`

`Paragraph 10: [theoretical or practical implication] → [an already-established finding] → [what follows, scoped to certainty AND to whether the finding bears on an actionable, decision-relevant quantity] → [register selected from: hazard-management-prescriptive / generalized-stakeholder-category / methodological-technological / research-agenda / data-adoption-infrastructure — treat all five as correct outcomes of proper calibration, not a strength hierarchy]`

`Paragraph 11: [closing] → [EITHER reconnect to the paper's overall framing/contribution (possibly broadened to a policy-relevance or field-relevance statement), OR issue a forward-research call, OR end on a precisely scoped, honestly stated limitation with no further synthesis] → [transition to a formal Conclusion section, where one exists separately from the Discussion; where Discussion and Conclusion are fused, this paragraph performs both functions at once]`

*(Note, extended at n=15: not every study needs all thirteen paragraph-types [the original eleven plus 2a and the discordance-as-supporting-evidence variant of 5]. Concordance-checking and mechanism-explanation can swap order; implication can fuse with the mechanism paragraph that supports it; the final paragraph is a genuine three-way choice. New at n=15: where the paper has no section literally named "Discussion" — a legitimate, observed structural choice — this skeleton's content still needs to appear SOMEWHERE in the paper, most likely distributed between the tail of the Results section and a Conclusions section performing double duty; do not assume the presence or absence of a "Discussion" heading tells you whether the paper is doing the rhetorical work this blueprint describes.)*

### Checklist of implicit rules (derived from what the fifteen papers consistently do; evidence strength stated for each, with corrections and strengthenings from the 9-paper version flagged explicitly)

1. **Never state a mechanism in the paper's own voice if the study's design cannot support that mechanism being causally demonstrated — attribute it explicitly using a reporting verb.** *(Was 6/9 (67%); now 8/15 (53%) — a genuine proportional weakening, explained by this batch's heavier representation of instrument-comparison and scenario-modeling designs, which need this move less often because their mechanisms sit within their own directly measured data. Still applies wherever the design-contingency condition holds; the rule's scope, not its strength when applicable, has changed.)*
2. **Never let a discordant or null finding pass without comment.** *(Was 7/9 (78%); now 12/15 (80%) — essentially unchanged in proportion, the framework's strongest comparative-handling rule alongside rule 3. New at n=15: the strongest form of this rule is to convert a well-explained discordance into active supporting evidence for the preferred result, not merely to explain it away — see rule 12 below.)*
3. **Do not save all limitations for a single terminal paragraph — place each limitation immediately after the specific claim it qualifies.** *(Was 8/9 (89%); now 14/15 (93%) — the single strongest, most confidently generalizable rule in the entire two-round checklist, strengthening on every re-test. Li remains the sole exception, for the same structural, not rhetorical, reason identified at n=9.)*
4. **Do NOT assume the Discussion must end on something other than a limitation.** *(Was a new rule at n=9, replacing a retracted one, evidenced 3/9. At n=15, re-tested against the correct 11-paper standalone base: 4/11 — essentially the same ~35-40% rate. CONFIRMED, not merely unchanged — a second independent sample landing at the same proportion is stronger evidence than the original single sample, that this is a stable, real minority convention rather than a sampling artifact.)*
5. **Match hedge strength to evidentiary basis, paragraph by paragraph, and calibrate multiple candidate explanations for the same finding independently within a single paragraph.** *(Now evidenced 4/15 with unusually rich instances — Cavalié's multiply-hedged creep-trigger chain and Dini et al.'s doubly-hedged cross-site mechanism explanation both extend the practice beyond the original two-instance base.)*
6. **When multiple prior studies are available, prefer citing several independent sources for convergent support — but a single close comparator can legitimately be reused for both concordance and discordance within or across paragraphs, provided each use is clearly distinguished.** *(The dual/multi-use practice is no longer a 1/9 idiosyncrasy — now 3/15, with Dini et al. providing the corpus's richest example, reusing two different citations across multiple paragraphs for genuinely distinct purposes each time.)*
7. **A genuine, unresolved anomaly should be stated as exactly that.** *(Unchanged in kind; distinguished cleanly from rule 8's rival-hypothesis-elimination move, as at n=9.)*
8. **When a plausible rival explanation for a finding exists and can be checked against a specific, stated ground, check it explicitly and report the outcome.** *(Was 4/9 (44%); now 5/15 papers (33%), 8 total instances given Dini et al.'s four. A genuine proportional weakening as a per-paper rate, but a strengthening in evidenced depth — the rule is now understood, with much richer illustration, as strongly design-contingent: near-default in single-event/single-case attribution designs, largely absent and not missed elsewhere.)*
9. **Practical or policy implications should be derived only from already-established claims, and the implication's register should track whether the finding bears on a decision-relevant, actionable quantity.** *(Was 7/9 (78%); now 10/15 (67%) — still clears two-thirds. The register qualification, tentative at n=9, is now confirmed across five independent instances spanning five distinct registers (Strozzi and Dini: methodological/research-agenda; Cavalié: generalized-stakeholder; Barthelemy: specific-actionable; Martins: data-adoption) — this is the checklist's clearest example of a qualification becoming a core, load-bearing part of the rule rather than remaining a secondary caveat.)*
10. **When a finding is favorable to the paper's own method or model, benchmark it against specific, named external results with specific numbers.** *(Unchanged in kind; Strozzi's velocity-stratified internal-benchmarking (D1) demonstrates the same discipline applies just as rigorously to internal cross-instrument comparisons as to external-literature or internal-ablation ones.)*
11. **When a finding could be over-generalized across the study's own internal heterogeneity, include an explicit boundary-setting statement.** *(Unchanged; none of the 6 new papers centers on a multi-site/multi-subgroup heterogeneity claim as directly as Yuen's two-delta comparison or Bendoni's three-city comparison — Bendoni's per-city organizing structure functions similarly in spirit but was scored under A2/A4a rather than as a dedicated boundary-setting rule instance.)*
12. **Close the Discussion by whichever of three moves is most honest given what was actually resolved — CORRECTED at n=9, CONFIRMED at n=15 on an independent 4-paper standalone sub-sample landing at materially the same proportion as the original 7-paper base.** *(No further correction needed; this is now the checklist's second most robustly evidenced finding, after rule 3.)*
13. **NEW at n=15 — When a paper's own predictor and its validation target share a structural dependency, name the dependency explicitly and resolve it by reasoned argument or direct demonstration before or alongside the concordance claim it could otherwise undermine.** *(2/15, Barthelemy and Martins — below any recurring threshold, but flagged as a distinct, higher-order discipline not visible at all in the 9-paper file, because none of those nine papers' designs created this specific risk. Directly relevant to any predictive model validated against a target that is not fully independent of the model's own inputs.)*
14. **NEW at n=15 — Where a discordance's cause is well-understood and independently checkable, state explicitly that the well-explained disagreement increases confidence in the preferred result, not merely that it does not undermine it.** *(1/15 clean instance, Martins — far below threshold, but named because it represents the strongest point on the discordance-handling spectrum documented across either round of this analysis, one step beyond rule 2's baseline requirement and a genuinely more assertive move than anything the 9-paper file's A2 discussion described.)*
